campaign/gazetteer/waterdeep/castle-ward.md:75: - Hook: festival processions converge on the Market, and Ammalia Cassalanter runs a charity booth near the Castle Ward entrance to it. Skeemo's potion drop to the lady in the purple cloak happens beside the God Catcher.
campaign/gazetteer/waterdeep/field-ward.md:13: > - The Cassalanters' Field Ward charity feeds hundreds. The **Cassalanter Villa** structure doc has the Waukeentide Charity Ball raise money for Field Ward reconstruction and draws the Founders' Day feast guests from the ward. The **Sea Maidens Faire** structure doc has the Doom Raiders name a private animal-feed supplier in the ward. The **Faction Outposts** structure doc names the ward once, for the chandler's cellar on Aveen Street.
campaign/gazetteer/waterdeep/field-ward.md:27: - **Services:** None from the city. The ward "has no sewer system and isn't served by the Dungsweepers' Guild" (Dragon Heist), and the Guild of Chandlers and Lamplighters lights the city's streets "excepting the Field Ward and the most dangerous areas of the Dock Ward". Three independent soup houses feed about four hundred people a day, and the Endshift Tavern is the one place Dragon Heist recommends for help. Ammalia Cassalanter's charity feeds hundreds, and the Halls of Justice fund a legal aid office for residents who cannot afford a hearing.
campaign/gazetteer/waterdeep/field-ward.md:40: > - The Cassalanters use the Field Ward as a source of victims and a cover for their cult. Ammalia's charity picks the one hundred poor guests for the Founders' Day feast on Flamerule 1, where ninety-nine cups of wine carry midnight tears, and the guests die within 24 hours, scattered across the city.
campaign/gazetteer/waterdeep/field-ward.md:41: > - Victoro Cassalanter has told a hired agent, Gysheer Omfreys, to keep the Field Ward frightened and the Watch busy elsewhere. She called the spined devils in Twelvedog Court for that purpose, and she has spent a tenday readying a cellar on Aveen Street for a larger working. The chandler's cellar on the Field Ward side opens into the Asmodean shrine on the North Ward side (see the **North Ward** Secret).
campaign/gazetteer/waterdeep/field-ward.md:72: > - The summoner is Gysheer Omfreys, a Tethyrian woman in her forties and a hired agent of Victoro Cassalanter. A chalk sigil at the alley mouth holds the devils to the court, and wiping it sends them back. Her note carries the initials "V.C.", and her day-book names the Aveen Street cellar. She has no Notable Figures page.

Dragon Heist says the Cassalanter Villa's household staff live "in dingy homes in the Dock Ward or the Field Ward" and arrive before the family wakes. The same text has the Founders' Day pavilions at the villa fill with "one hundred of Waterdeep's poorest residents", most of them brought from the Field Ward.

campaign/gazetteer/waterdeep/sea-ward.md:32: - **Architecture:** Palatial homes behind garden walls, many built around the remains of old pirate and warlord fortresses. The Cassalanter Villa's white walls and crimson roof and turrets. The House of Wonder's ornate towers and mosaics. The Tower of the Moon, rising seventy-five feet above the street. The Field of Triumph, over five stories tall.
campaign/gazetteer/waterdeep/sea-ward.md:33: - **Economy:** Rents that only the rich can pay. Banking through the Cassalanter Counting House, trading houses, vintners and gem merchants, and the arena's crowds. Guild law applies as everywhere in the city.

They mean to poison ninety-nine guests at the Founders' Day feast on Flamerule 1 to buy out the twins' souls. The villa is mapped in **Cassalanter Villa**.
campaign/gazetteer/waterdeep/sea-ward.md:40: > - Nobody in the ward knows. Every faction suspects the family because it is too nice to be this rich, and none knows about the pact. campaign/gazetteer/waterdeep/sea-ward.md:69: **Cassalanter Villa** — Noble estate
campaign/gazetteer/waterdeep/sea-ward.md:70: - Stark white walls and a gleaming crimson roof with turrets stand out even among the Sea Ward's other opulent estates. A tall white brick wall surrounds green gardens of hedges and water features, and one wrought-iron gate carries the family crest, a green Y overlapping a stylized goose being fed. Armored guards flank the gate. The campaign journal gives the mansion four stories above ground and a topiary garden kept for two generations, and Ammalia's butterfly garden lies at the back.
campaign/gazetteer/waterdeep/sea-ward.md:71: - Victoro and Ammalia Cassalanter live here, and the family hosts public galas during Waukeentide (Tarsakh 1 to 10).

campaign/guides/factions/11-cassalanters.md:9: If the party agreed to help, the Cassalanters go quiet. With the characters in the field as their agents, drawing attention to themselves risks unraveling the arrangement. The Cassalanters may dispatch Seffia's team if the party requests backup or if they need to insert themselves without appearing to act. Willifort keeps tabs on the characters from a distance, reporting to Ammalia, and the family's response apparatus holds.
campaign/guides/factions/11-cassalanters.md:15: Two teams carry out Cassalanter field operations. Seffia's team is precision work; Willifort's is a blunt instrument the family draws only once discretion has failed.
campaign/guides/factions/11-cassalanters.md:35: *Area 6 — Mirror of the Fiend.* An obsidian-framed mirror that deals 1d6 damage per round to most creatures and heals 1d6 per round for fiends. A carved Cassalanter noble sigil marks the back. Any character who examines the mirror and makes a successful DC 13 Intelligence (History) check recognizes it as a Waterdhavian noble marking; on a result of 16 or higher, the character identifies the family.
campaign/guides/factions/11-cassalanters.md:43: The apprentice wizard in Area 2 carries *Instructions from Lord Cassalanter*, directing that the next pair of summoned Imps be sent to surveil the Sea Maidens Faire.

If the sentry Imp escapes, the Cassalanters know exactly what was taken. **Founders' Day Clock (Flamerule 1): 3–5 days.**

campaign/guides/factions/11-cassalanters.md:107: - Notable Figures: **Victoro Cassalanter**, **Ammalia Cassalanter**, **Willifort Crowelle**, **Seffia Naelryke**, **Arn Xalrondar**, **Tissina Khyret**, **Laiba "Nana" Rosse**, **Jandar Chergoba**, **Rella Asp**, **Ivrala Sorn**, **Osvaldo Cassalanter**, **Caladorn Cassalanter**
campaign/guides/gm-guide/about-this-campaign.md:11: **Act III is four distinct heist genres.** Xanathar's Lair is dungeon infiltration; Cassalanter Villa is social penetration; Sea Maidens Faire is a nautical caper; Kolat Towers is a fortress assault. Each target is scoutable, plannable, and responsive to how thoroughly the party prepares.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/about-this-campaign.md:30: **Make It a Heist** means preparation changes the options. A party that cases Cassalanter Villa gets better entry options and knows where Willifort Crowelle is standing. A party that wings it still gets through, but improvises more of it. See [Design Notes](design-notes-running-the-campaign.md) for the five-step heist framework.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/about-this-campaign.md:70: Play the Cassalanters as people who love their children. Victoro and Ammalia made a catastrophic bargain, and Asmodeus took their eldest son the day they signed. Osvaldo was nine. He is a chain devil now, chained in a soundproofed attic, and his mother still comes to read to him. Their love is real; their plan is monstrous; the gap between those two things is the whole point. When players understand it, some will want to help, some will want to stop the poison and let the twins die, and some will be unable to choose. All of those responses are valid. Play them straight: sympathetic motives, genuine evil, no winking at the camera. Trust the players to sit with the discomfort.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/about-this-campaign.md:78: > - Osvaldo Cassalanter was a person before he was a chain devil. He was nine when he was taken. His mother still visits him.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/adventure-summary.md:11: | III | The Heists | 4–7 | Faction Outposts, Xanathar's Lair, Cassalanter Villa, Sea Maidens Faire, Kolat Towers | 16–28 | Can the PCs recover all three Eyes before any faction assembles them? |
campaign/guides/gm-guide/adventure-summary.md:29: Five quests, four heist targets, no fixed order. The party holds the Stone and needs three Eyes to open the Vault. Faction Outposts provides the intelligence to plan. Three of the four lair heists hold an Eye; Kolat Towers holds Manshoon. Each target is built as a genuine operation: scoutable, plannable, and responsive to how thoroughly the party prepares. Xanathar's Lair is a dungeon infiltration into the criminal undercity. Cassalanter Villa is social penetration into a noble household with a cult temple beneath it. The Sea Maidens Faire is a nautical caper aboard three ships. Kolat Towers is a fortress assault against Manshoon's extradimensional sanctum. Two clocks run underneath all of it: the Faire departs Tarsakh 20 and Founders' Day falls Flamerule 1. Act III is the bulk of the campaign. Thorough planning pays off; the structure supports improvisation.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/bonds-and-flaws.md:52: > | 3 | **Cassalanter Villa**: the Cassalanters are parents who did something unforgivable for the right reasons. |
campaign/guides/gm-guide/bonds-and-flaws.md:54: > | 5 | **Cassalanter Villa**: are the Cassalanter children worth the cost of saving them? |
campaign/guides/gm-guide/bonds-and-flaws.md:56: > | 7 | The lair heists (**Xanathar's Lair**, **Cassalanter Villa**, **Sea Maidens Faire**, **Kolat Towers**): none of them can be done alone. |
campaign/guides/gm-guide/design-notes-running-the-campaign.md:25: We apply a five-step framework to every Act III lair: Identify the Score, Gather Information, Surveillance, Preparation, and Operation. The framework solves a specific problem. The original design rewards players who engage with faction politics but does almost nothing for players who ask good questions, case the target, and plan their approach. Under the heist framework, planning pays off. A party that does careful surveillance of Cassalanter Villa gets better entry options and intelligence on Willifort's detect thoughts range. A party that wings it still has a path through, but they spend more of the operation improvising rather than executing a plan they built.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/design-notes-running-the-campaign.md:27: The four lairs run as four distinct genres. Xanathar's Lair is dungeon infiltration: break into an underground complex with a beholder at the center. Cassalanter Villa is social infiltration: work within a functioning household whose owners are watching you back. Sea Maidens Faire is a caper: the thing that's supposed to go wrong goes wrong in the first act, and you adapt in real time. Kolat Towers is a raid: Manshoon isn't a puzzle, he's an obstacle, and the question is whether you can take him. Different player instincts dominate different genres, so the heist phase plays differently from session to session rather than feeling like four iterations of the same map.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/design-notes-running-the-campaign.md:67: The contract Victoro and Ammalia Cassalanter signed with Asmodeus specifies its terms: 999,999 gold pieces and ninety-nine souls, delivered before the twins' ninth birthday on Flamerule 11. The birthday is the date the contract comes due.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/design-notes-running-the-campaign.md:69: Founders' Day is different. Ammalia chose Flamerule 1 as her operational window for the soul collection. The plan: invite one hundred of Waterdeep's poorest to a charitable feast on the villa's lawn pavilions, administer midnight tears through ninety-nine cups of wine, and let the guests go home. They die the next day, scattered across the city, with no visible connection to the Cassalanters. Founders' Day works as a cover because a civic celebration makes Ammalia's invitation to the poor unremarkable, because her established charity work makes the feast plausible, and because ten days before the birthday is the last window that still allows time to verify the contract terms are met.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/design-notes-running-the-campaign.md:73: Why Founders' Day rather than some other date? Because the feast's civic resonance is not incidental. A celebration of Waterdeep's founding, attended by its most vulnerable citizens, provides the perfect cover for a Cassalanter sacrifice. Ammalia is not blind to the symbolism. She's spent years learning not to look at it too closely.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/design-notes-running-the-campaign.md:123: - Brimel Crestfall (Cassalanter household steward; **The Theater's Back Room** informant) — single-mission NPC; no profile
campaign/guides/gm-guide/design-notes-running-the-campaign.md:124: - Florette Cressyn (Cassalanter watcher; **The Theater's Back Room** antagonist) — single-mission NPC; no profile
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:43: Inter-faction activity continues off-screen. If the party spends three sessions pursuing Cassalanter outposts, Xanathar and Manshoon haven't been idle. Keep a one-line answer ready for what each faction did during that time. When PCs canvass contacts, read Watch reports, or press informants, they may turn up traces of that off-screen activity. The world feels inhabited rather than scripted when the factions are visibly busy with their own agendas.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:67: The Cassalanters run a parallel track, largely independent of the others. They aren't competing with the other factions for the Stone; they intend to wait until someone activates it and reveals the vault's location, then move on the vault directly. Their outpost network is focused on gathering gold and ritual components. Cassalanter imps conduct ongoing surveillance of the Sea Maidens Faire, but Jarlaxle's counter-intelligence hasn't detected it yet.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:82: **The Cassalanters** retreat to social and legal pressure before infernal force. Their first response to a party that has become a problem is Watch complaints, noble pressure, guild sanctions, and quiet damage to the PCs' reputation in the city. What they want for standing down is discretion: a party that will stay out of their affairs and never speak of the soul pact. Ammalia Cassalanter is more comfortable wielding Waterdeep's social machinery than facing direct confrontation. What they remember is anyone who has learned too much.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:84: They can also be negotiated out of the Grand Game entirely. The vault gold is a means, not an end. Everything they have done since signing Asmodeus's contract has aimed at buying their children's freedom before the twins' ninth birthday, and once a party discovers the full truth and approaches them directly, the social mask drops. Victoro's polished banker act vanishes and a frightened father takes his place. Ammalia weeps. They will take any genuine alternative the party offers, immediately and without negotiation. Two exits from the Game are available. In the cooperative path, the party secures the vault gold on their behalf; the Cassalanters exit satisfied, but every coin from Neverember's hoard goes to Asmodeus and the party receives nothing. In the contract path, the mace of disruption, recovered from Caladorn Cassalanter's ghost in the villa's family crypt, strikes the Asmodeus statue in A7, severs the infernal contract, and renders the gold irrelevant; the children are freed without payment, but Asmodean retribution follows within days. Both routes require reaching **Cassalanter Villa** first. The full resolution mechanics, including the sacrifice clause that runs parallel to the gold payment, are there.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:100: | **Whitehawk** | 10 | Once per quest, two Order Scouts watch a suspect or location you name for three days and give you a written report. Before each quest's main operation you receive a *Potion of Healing* and a *Potion of Greater Healing*. You choose one Uncommon magic item from a table of four. Temple priests of the Triad cast spells of 2nd level or lower for you at no cost. Your contact also gives you the Order's dossier on the family it has watched longest. The Scouts watch and report from outside, and they never enter a private building or follow a target underground. The four items are the Sentinel Shield, the Cloak of Protection, the Brooch of Shielding and the Periapt of Wound Closure. Components under 100 gp are covered. The dossier is a suspicion file that proves nothing, and Savra says "the family" until a player names a Cassalanter. |

campaign/guides/gm-guide/structural-rules.md:49: | Tarsakh 1–10 | Waukeentide | Trade festival; Cassalanter social events peak; cover for Cassalanter Villa surveillance |
campaign/guides/gm-guide/structural-rules.md:51: | Flamerule 1 | Founders' Day | **Hard deadline:** Cassalanter charity feast; 99 cups of midnight tears served; guests die within 24 hours; stop the feast to stop the sacrifice |
campaign/guides/gm-guide/structural-rules.md:52: | Flamerule 11 | Cassalanter twins' ninth birthday | Contract deadline: 999,999 gp and 99 souls due to Asmodeus; the twins are forfeit if the terms go unmet or unbroken |
campaign/guides/gm-guide/structural-rules.md:58: **Flamerule 1** is the sacrifice window. The Cassalanters host a charity dinner at Cassalanter Villa; 99 guests receive cups laced with midnight tears and die within 24 hours. The party must stop the feast to prevent the sacrifice. Arriving after the dinner ends changes nothing.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/structural-rules.md:60: **Flamerule 11** follows ten days later — the contract's due date. Parties who destroyed the contract during **Cassalanter Villa** don't face this deadline. Those who didn't have ten days after Founders' Day to find another resolution.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/structural-rules.md:65: > Two deadlines, ten days apart. Founders' Day (Flamerule 1) is the feast, where ninety-nine guests are poisoned; it is the party's real deadline. The twins' ninth birthday (Flamerule 11) is when the contract comes due. A party that stopped the feast still has those ten days to settle the twins' fate through one of the **Cassalanter Villa** resolutions.
campaign/guides/trollskull-manor/01-overview.md:13: **Tarsakh 20 — Sea Maidens Faire departure.** Jarlaxle departs Waterdeep aboard the *Sea Maidens Faire* fleet at dawn on Tarsakh 20. After that date, the **Sea Maidens Faire** alliance path is no longer available and the faction relationship defaults to adversarial. This is the **Sea Maidens Faire** deadline only; it has no connection to **Cassalanter Villa**.

campaign/locations/cassalanter-villa/01-entrance-hall.md:3: A two-story foyer with marble floors and a grand staircase curving to the second floor. The walls are hung with Cassalanter ancestor portraits — Caladorn Cassalanter is prominently placed near the stair, silver-haired and armored in the heraldic style of the North. The room smells of fresh flowers and warm candle wax.
campaign/locations/cassalanter-villa/01-entrance-hall.md:5: ### Normal — Fireball Dinner / Cassalanter Villa Day Operations
campaign/locations/cassalanter-villa/01-entrance-hall.md:9: During Fireball (ev-06), the party arrives here by Ammalia's invitation. Willifort takes coats, offers wine, and escorts the party to C9 (dining room). He reads the party during this transit. The Social Exposure System begins at Stage 1.
campaign/locations/cassalanter-villa/01-entrance-hall.md:13: ### Heist — Cassalanter Villa Social Event Configuration
campaign/locations/cassalanter-villa/01-entrance-hall.md:21: Doppelganger, tiefling disguise. Combat behavior: Multiattack in his natural form; flees through the service corridor if reduced below 15 HP. His absence triggers an immediate household response — Ammalia checks on him within 15 minutes of any gap in his presence.
campaign/locations/cassalanter-villa/01-entrance-hall.md:23: During normal operations, Willifort reads surface thoughts passively. A creature actively controlling surface thoughts must make a DC 13 Charisma (Deception) check per interaction with him. Failure: he flags a concerning thought to Ammalia but does not immediately escalate — the party advances to Stage 2. If a creature is immune to *Charm Person* or has active mind-protection (e.g., *ring of mind shielding*), Willifort notices the blank spot: he cannot force a reading, but the absence registers as anomalous. He notes it and does not react unless other triggers accumulate.
campaign/locations/cassalanter-villa/02-garden-mudroom.md:9: ### Heist — Cassalanter Villa Social Event Configuration
campaign/locations/cassalanter-villa/03-reading-room.md:3: Ground floor, east wing. A comfortable room with leather chairs and bookshelves filling two walls. A black dragon hunting trophy is mounted above the fireplace — old and well-maintained, the taxidermy work from another era. Victoro receives guests here during daylight hours when no formal event is scheduled.
campaign/locations/cassalanter-villa/03-reading-room.md:5: A private journal rests on the side table beside the leftmost chair. Victoro reads it here. He sometimes forgets to take it with him.
campaign/locations/cassalanter-villa/03-reading-room.md:14: > *"The Cassalanter mausoleum. Inform Y.G."*
campaign/locations/cassalanter-villa/03-reading-room.md:17: The first entry confirms the Vault of Dragons' gold is the financial component of the buy-out clause. The second is a name ("Y.G." — identifiable as Yalah Gralhund with a DC 14 Intelligence (History) check, cross-referencing the Gralhund family and their Sea Ward connections). The third confirms Victoro understands the Stone's nature — the journal predates the fireball. He has been planning around the Stone for longer than the party has known it existed.
campaign/locations/cassalanter-villa/03-reading-room.md:21: ### Heist — Cassalanter Villa Social Event / Day Infiltration
campaign/locations/cassalanter-villa/03-reading-room.md:23: During social events, Victoro entertains guests here in rotation with C9 (dining room). He holds a wine glass and makes banking small talk. The journal is on the side table unless Victoro is in the room, in which case it is closed on the table beside him.
campaign/locations/cassalanter-villa/03-reading-room.md:25: During day infiltration (non-event windows), the reading room may be occupied by Victoro or empty. If empty, the journal is present — same content as above. If Victoro is present: the journal is on his person or on a locked writing desk that has not yet been opened. The writing desk is not separately locked — it is just a desk.
campaign/locations/cassalanter-villa/03-reading-room.md:27: The black dragon trophy: recognizable by any character with DC 12 Intelligence (Nature) as young adult, female, killed by a piercing weapon. The Cassalanter family crest does not include a dragon — this trophy postdates the heraldry. No mechanical function; it establishes that Victoro or an ancestor hunted dragons, a detail that reads differently once the party knows the Vault is guarded by one.
campaign/locations/cassalanter-villa/04-victoros-office.md:1: ﻿# C6 — Victoro's Office
campaign/locations/cassalanter-villa/04-victoros-office.md:5: **Lock:** DC 17 Dexterity (Thieves' Tools), or Victoro's key. There is no key hanging in the rest of the villa — Victoro carries it.
campaign/locations/cassalanter-villa/04-victoros-office.md:7: A helmed horror stands in the corner, motionless. It does not react to anyone entering the room. It activates the moment any creature other than Victoro or Ammalia touches the desk.
campaign/locations/cassalanter-villa/04-victoros-office.md:11: The office is empty when Victoro is receiving guests or at a social event. Lissette the upstairs maid cleans the room during midmorning on Tuesdays and Fridays, using a key that Victoro leaves on a hook outside the door during those windows (automatic, predictable).
campaign/locations/cassalanter-villa/04-victoros-office.md:15: ### Heist — Cassalanter Villa Infiltration
campaign/locations/cassalanter-villa/04-victoros-office.md:17: **The Helmed Horror.** AC 20, HP 60. It pursues the triggering creature throughout the villa but does not leave the building. It is immune to fire, lightning, and the frightened condition. It attacks until the triggering creature is dead or incapacitated, then returns to its corner. The deactivation word is unknown to the party; Victoro speaks it in Infernal. No source within the villa will yield this word — the helmed horror is fought, fled, or endured.
campaign/locations/cassalanter-villa/04-victoros-office.md:23: No check required to find the desk's surface papers. Thirteen loan contracts — the Cassalanter banking leverage over Waterdeep's guilds. Names, sums, terms. A party member with DC 12 Intelligence (History) can identify that these contracts represent effective control over the Chandlers', Coopers', and Carpenters' guilds, among others.
campaign/locations/cassalanter-villa/04-victoros-office.md:27: Also in the hidden compartment: the **Cassalanter Report on the Grand Game**. This document is a hand-written summary of the current faction competition for the Stone of Golorr, prepared by Victoro for his own reference. It references Manshoon's operations directly, identifies Kolat Towers by name and location, describes Zhentarim splinter movements in the Castle Ward, and includes the note: *"The wizard who calls himself Manshoon guards his fortress with a force field that admits only those he has keyed."* This is the Three Clue Rule path to Kolat Towers — Kolat Towers.
campaign/locations/cassalanter-villa/04-victoros-office.md:29: A second copy of the Cassalanter Report on the Grand Game is kept in C22 (Ammalia's study), ensuring that parties who do not reach C6 still have access to the Kolat Towers clue.
campaign/locations/cassalanter-villa/05-kitchen.md:13: **Turning Jandar:** Victoro holds a 3,000 gp financial obligation over him, converted to cult service three years ago. Jandar hates what he does. DC 12 Charisma (Persuasion) with an offer of protection turns him; DC 10 if the party can resolve the debt or credibly promise to. He provides: kitchen schedules, which deliveries access the cellar, and the location of the wine cellar's false wall. He does not know the Siamorphe sun passphrase for C27 — only that the cellar has "a funny door that the family's guests use on certain nights."
campaign/locations/cassalanter-villa/05-kitchen.md:15: ### Heist — Cassalanter Villa Social Event / Founders' Day Configuration
campaign/locations/cassalanter-villa/06-ballroom.md:11: ### Heist — Cassalanter Villa Social Event Configuration
campaign/locations/cassalanter-villa/06-ballroom.md:17: **Ammalia's Pattern.** During social events, Ammalia rotates through C13 (ballroom), C15 (banquet hall), and C25 (butterfly garden) on a twenty-minute cycle. If the party is tracking her position for access to C22, her C13 presence is the departure point — she moves upstairs to C22 only at the event's end.
campaign/locations/cassalanter-villa/07-ammalias-study.md:1: ﻿# C22 — Ammalia's Study
campaign/locations/cassalanter-villa/07-ammalias-study.md:3: Third floor. A private study with a lectern, a writing desk, and shelves of correspondence sorted by year. The room is cleaner than a working study should be — papers filed, surfaces clear, everything in its place. Ammalia spends long hours here. The door is locked.
campaign/locations/cassalanter-villa/07-ammalias-study.md:5: **Lock:** DC 18 Dexterity (Thieves' Tools), or Ammalia's key. The key never leaves her person.
campaign/locations/cassalanter-villa/07-ammalias-study.md:11: Ammalia is in this room during late mornings and afternoons when no event is scheduled. She does not receive visitors here. If Lissette the upstairs maid attempts to clean the room, Ammalia opens the door briefly, takes whatever laundry is waiting in the hall, and closes the door again. The key never leaves her hand during this exchange.
campaign/locations/cassalanter-villa/07-ammalias-study.md:13: If the party accesses the room without Ammalia present:
campaign/locations/cassalanter-villa/07-ammalias-study.md:16: - The commune ritual Ammalia uses to contact Asmodeus through a pit fiend proxy, written in her handwriting
campaign/locations/cassalanter-villa/07-ammalias-study.md:20: **The Desk.** Ammalia's spellbook in a locked drawer (DC 16 Thieves' Tools). Ten vials of midnight tears poison on a small wooden rack beside the spellbook — unlabeled, clear liquid, tasteless, effect onset 24 hours. Any character who succeeds on a DC 15 Intelligence (Medicine) check identifies the substance.
campaign/locations/cassalanter-villa/07-ammalias-study.md:22: **The Cassalanter Report on the Grand Game (a second copy).** Same document as the one in C6 — Victoro's hand-written summary identifying Kolat Towers and Manshoon's force field. Ensuring parties who reach C22 but not C6 still find the Kolat Towers clue.
campaign/locations/cassalanter-villa/07-ammalias-study.md:26: ### Heist — Cassalanter Villa Social Event Configuration
campaign/locations/cassalanter-villa/07-ammalias-study.md:28: During social events, Ammalia is in the ballroom rotation (C13/C15/C25 on a twenty-minute cycle). She returns to C22 only at the event's close. The study is unoccupied, the door locked, the trapdoor to C24 accessible to any party that has reached this room.
campaign/locations/cassalanter-villa/08-osvaldos-prison.md:3: The attic. Accessible via the stair from C23 (Ammalia's study trapdoor), or through a locked exterior door on the attic level from the third-floor landing.
campaign/locations/cassalanter-villa/08-osvaldos-prison.md:11: ### Heist — Cassalanter Villa Only
campaign/locations/cassalanter-villa/08-osvaldos-prison.md:15: > This room has no Normal section. It is inaccessible during Fireball — the attic is sealed and forbidden to all household staff. The exterior door's lock (DC 19) is beyond any non-plot access path in Fireball. Access occurs only during the Cassalanter Villa operation.
campaign/locations/cassalanter-villa/08-osvaldos-prison.md:23: **Two imps in spider form** cling to the rafters above the ring. They are passive observers during normal attic operations, reporting to Ammalia through a one-way telepathic bond. They attack if Osvaldo is directly threatened or if the party attempts to dispel the containment wards.
campaign/locations/cassalanter-villa/08-osvaldos-prison.md:29: *Dispel the wards (dispel magic):* The wards collapse. Osvaldo attacks — Multiattack, chains lashing, chain devil statistics. Then, mid-swing, he stops. He weeps. He looks at his own hands. The voice says: "I'm sorry." He dies. His form dissolves into black ichor that smells of brimstone and ink. Two imps immediately report the death to Ammalia (the telepathic bond severs with a psychic snap — she receives it wherever she is in the villa). Ammalia moves toward C24 in the following round.
campaign/locations/cassalanter-villa/08-osvaldos-prison.md:35: Lissette the upstairs maid mentions hearing Ammalia crying after she visits "the top floor." The twins' caretaker Nana Rosse knows "something terrible" lives in the attic. Neither has been inside.
campaign/locations/cassalanter-villa/09-butterfly-garden.md:5: The Cassalanters maintain this garden as a social amenity. Nobles duel here for entertainment, under the shade of flowering trees, while other guests place wagers. Ammalia presents it as a conservation project — birds rescued from the docks, butterflies bred from imported specimens.
campaign/locations/cassalanter-villa/09-butterfly-garden.md:9: Six imps in raven form perch in the garden. They move between the trees, the wall, and occasionally the garden's wrought-iron benches. They observe all movement through the garden and around the villa's perimeter, reporting to Ammalia through a one-way telepathic bond. She receives impressions — not words.
campaign/locations/cassalanter-villa/09-butterfly-garden.md:13: **Killing an imp:** The telepathic bond severs with a psychic snap. Ammalia receives the sensation immediately wherever she is. One dead imp: she registers an anomaly and sends a servant to check the garden within five minutes. Three or more dead: she advances the Social Exposure System by one stage and moves toward the garden herself.
campaign/locations/cassalanter-villa/09-butterfly-garden.md:15: Marchello the gardener tends the garden at dawn and dusk. He is aware that the ravens watch people ("Regular birds don't watch you"). He has not connected this to anything supernatural — Ammalia told him they were rescue birds from the docks.
campaign/locations/cassalanter-villa/09-butterfly-garden.md:17: ### Heist — Cassalanter Villa Social Event Configuration

Combat behavior: *Charm Person* (5th-level, up to 5 targets), *Dominate Person* on most dangerous combatant, *Counterspell* defensively. Arrives to aid Victoro within 2 rounds of hearing combat.

campaign/quests/act-i/finding-floon/design-notes.md:15: ***Cassalanter Name-Drop Through Renaer.*** Renaer's aside about the Cassalanters' property acquisitions near the Brandath tombs sits unexamined in player notes until Cassalanter Villa. He raises it as an oddity he can't explain. A first-level party has no use for it yet, and none is offered.
campaign/quests/act-i/trollskull-alley/design-notes.md:27: ## Ammalia Cassalanter's Introduction
campaign/quests/act-i/trollskull-alley/design-notes.md:29: **Her Warmth Must Be Genuine.** The temptation is to signal the Cassalanters as villains through dramatic irony: slightly too-bright smiles, odd conversational choices, a moment of coldness beneath the grace. Resist this entirely. Ammalia Cassalanter is a warm, generous, politically sophisticated woman who is also a cult leader preparing to sacrifice children to Asmodeus. The warmth is how she has always functioned in the world; the diabolism is a choice she made under duress and has not stepped back from. If players suspect her at the Grand Opening, it should be because they are suspicious people, not because the text gave them a signal.
campaign/quests/act-i/trollskull-alley/design-notes.md:31: The dramatic irony of Cassalanter Villa, watching a woman they genuinely liked negotiate a devil's contract, requires that the liking be earned honestly in Trollskull Alley.
campaign/quests/act-i/trollskull-alley/ev-06-the-grand-opening.md:10: > - The Cassalanter Bank Hook precedes the opening by two days: wine delivery, card from the house
campaign/quests/act-i/trollskull-alley/ev-06-the-grand-opening.md:11: > - Opening night has a full guest list including faction contacts, neighbors, and Ammalia Cassalanter
campaign/quests/act-i/trollskull-alley/ev-06-the-grand-opening.md:19: > Cross-ref the **Trollskull Manor** guide (Staff and Hiring, Fame, Revenue), the **Ammalia Cassalanter** page in Notable Figures.
campaign/quests/act-i/trollskull-alley/ev-06-the-grand-opening.md:31: ### The Cassalanter Bank Hook
campaign/quests/act-i/trollskull-alley/ev-06-the-grand-opening.md:33: When the party's renovation financing required a formal loan or credit arrangement, their paperwork passed through the **Cassalanter Counting House** (a Sea Ward banking operation with accounts throughout the North Ward). Two days before opening, a messenger delivers a cask of Athkatlan vintage wine with a card: *"From the house of Cassalanter. We look forward to patronizing your establishment."*

campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/design-notes.md:43: ***Frustration That Becomes Coherent.*** The Faire visit feels like a stall because it is one — the Gralhund nimblewright is not there. This is intentional. Following a real lead to a productive-but-incomplete result is exactly what good investigative fiction builds tolerance for. The payoff arrives the moment the party moves north and the detector changes behavior on Saerdoun Street. The intelligence gained at the Faire (the ledger, Jarlaxle's identity, the Cassalanter entry, the BD private meeting) makes the detour retroactively coherent.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/design-notes.md:47: ## The Cassalanter Dinner
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/design-notes.md:49: ***Bridging Trollskull Alley to Cassalanter Villa.*** The original campaign places the Cassalanters as Trollskull Alley patrons and then drops them entirely until the Cassalanter Villa heist, with no intervening contact. The dinner scene solves this pacing problem: it delivers the first approach with an agenda (Victoro at the table, not just Ammalia) while revealing nothing premature — no infernal pact, no children, no Asmodeus. The party's response becomes the Cassalanters' operational picture for Cassalanter Villa. Four response tracks produce four distinct starting states for the Cassalanter Villa infiltration.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/design-notes.md:51: ***Ammalia Is Not Performing.*** Her warmth is established behavior from Trollskull Alley and is not a deception. The design temptation is to make warm NPCs secretly cold and cold NPCs secretly warm — this undermines the party's ability to read the room. Ammalia is genuinely warm and that warmth is a negotiating position because she possesses it, not because she is manufacturing it. These are different things, and playing the distinction correctly changes the dinner's texture.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/design-notes.md:55: ***Non-Essential, Consequential.*** ev-06 and ev-07 cannot block the party's path to Gralhund Villa. But a party that pursues them gains: the death mark (characterization of Xanathar's Guild's sophistication), the Cassalanter-to-vault link through Kalain's account of Victoro's visit, the vault-opening method, and the unsigned Eyes letter confirming Neverember's deliberate separation of Stone and Eyes. These are among the most consequential intelligence finds in the campaign, gated behind exactly the investigative habits this quest tries to build: exhaustive canvassing, leaving contact information, following up on letters addressed to dead men.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/design-notes.md:61: ***The First Quest Where All Four Villain Factions Pursue the Same Object.*** Finding Floon establishes the Guild and the Zhentarim as adversaries. Trollskull Alley introduces the Cassalanters as patrons and BD as observers. Fireball is the first quest where all four reach for the same object at the same time in the same city. The design makes this concrete: Xanathar gazers in the alley, Zhent parallel investigation, BD conditional observers, Cassalanter condolence note and dinner invitation. These are not flavor — they are the Grand Game becoming visible for the first time.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-01-the-fireball.md:194: **Harpers — Mirt (Renown 1+):** Takes a Harper member aside privately at some point before the party reaches the Sea Maidens Faire. He suspects Cassalanter money was behind the Howling Hatred cult three years ago, because the Watch released two of its members after a quiet word from someone at Cassalanter Villa. He has no proof and wants some. He is sharing it now because he is worried, which is different from being certain.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-01-the-fireball.md:215: - **Cassalanters:** Flowers and a warm handwritten condolence note delivered to Trollskull Manor — Ammalia's handwriting. No return address on the card.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-03-the-nimblewright-hunt.md:71: > A party that goes directly to Gralhund Villa skips Scene 4's intelligence entirely. They do not learn Jarlaxle's identity, do not see the ledger, and do not know about the Cassalanter entry. Jarlaxle remains a background presence — aware, watching, not deployed. This is not a penalty. It is a realistic consequence of investigative depth: the party turned over fewer stones and Jarlaxle had less surface to react to.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-03-the-nimblewright-hunt.md:87: > **If Faire Visited First: False** — proceed directly to **Gralhund Villa: Gralhund Villa**. The quest transition is immediate. Fireball ends here for this party. The Cassalanter dinner (ev-05) and Dalakhar backtrack (ev-06, ev-07) are only available if the party delays before entering the estate — available windows are noted in Gralhund Villa's opening.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-04-the-sea-maidens-faire.md:91: | NW-03 | CR-03 | Marpenoth 12 | V. Cassalanter | Cassalanter Villa, Sea Ward | *VOID* |
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-04-the-sea-maidens-faire.md:98: **Notable entry:** NW-03, V. Cassalanter, Cassalanter Villa, Sea Ward — VOID. Notation: "Buyer withdrew — unspecified concerns re: detection." Victoro Cassalanter suspected the nimblewrights might contain surveillance devices. He was correct. The clairvoyance crystal reference numbers in the ledger are the key: each purchased nimblewright has a corresponding crystal recording what it witnesses. Victoro pulled his order when he worked this out. The Crystal Ball at the *Marpenoth* proves the point. This pays off in Cassalanter Villa.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-04-the-sea-maidens-faire.md:102: > #### The Voided Cassalanter Entry

campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-04-the-sea-maidens-faire.md:146: > The Cassalanter dinner (ev-05) and the Dalakhar backtrack (ev-06, ev-07) remain available before the party commits to entering the estate. Gralhund Villa's first beat covers the decision window.

campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-05-the-cassalanter-dinner.md:24: ### Cassalanter Villa — Arrival
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-05-the-cassalanter-dinner.md:28: ### Ammalia at the Table
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-05-the-cassalanter-dinner.md:32: **DC 14 Wisdom (Insight):** She is watching to see how the party handles kindness, not suspicion. She is forming an opinion. The result of that opinion is Victoro's problem. A failed check produces no false read — the GM does not say she seems deceptive. She simply seems warm.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-05-the-cassalanter-dinner.md:34: What she knows: the same as Victoro. She shares none of it. Her role at the table is to make the party comfortable enough that Victoro's question feels collaborative rather than predatory.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-05-the-cassalanter-dinner.md:36: ### Victoro's Pitch
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-05-the-cassalanter-dinner.md:38: After the first course, Victoro raises the fireball. He expresses condolences. He pauses, then:
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-05-the-cassalanter-dinner.md:44: **DC 16 Wisdom (Insight) on Victoro:** He is a man who made a decision he cannot unmake and has arranged his life carefully around it. This is accurate. It yields nothing specific — no pact, no children, no divine patron. A DC 14 read confirms he is telling the truth about what he is asking for; it does not reveal what he is not saying.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-05-the-cassalanter-dinner.md:46: **What the Cassalanters know and do not say:** Victoro knows Dalakhar worked for Neverember, the Stone is the key to a concealed vault, all three Eyes were separated from the Stone years ago, and the vault's physical entrance is in the Brandath family crypt. He withholds all of this. His goal tonight is assessment — whether the party is worth cultivating.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-05-the-cassalanter-dinner.md:54: **Consequence:** The Cassalanters learn where the investigation points. Victoro updates his operational picture. Entering Cassalanter Villa, he knows what the party found at Gralhund Villa and what they did there. Cassalanter response teams are positioned accordingly. Ammalia thanks them warmly. Victoro follows up within two days with a sealed letter summarizing what the Cassalanters have learned — no vault specifics; he is not foolish. The alliance track in Cassalanter Villa opens.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-05-the-cassalanter-dinner.md:60: **Consequence:** Victoro notes the restraint and interprets it as operational caution — a quality he respects. He does not press. He says: "Of course. We'll be available, when the time comes." Entering Cassalanter Villa, the party is treated as capable but unaligned. Neither warmth nor frost.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-05-the-cassalanter-dinner.md:64: The party agrees to Victoro's terms — they'll tell him when they find the Stone.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-05-the-cassalanter-dinner.md:66: **Consequence:** Same as Track A's follow-up letter, but Victoro's subsequent communication is more specific: he names the vault as "Lord Dagult's Melairkyn project" without elaboration, which confirms the party's prior knowledge if they visited Kalain. Entering Cassalanter Villa, the party has a document trail and a prior relationship. The alliance track opens with one step already taken.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-05-the-cassalanter-dinner.md:72: **Consequence:** Ammalia does not change expression. Victoro sees them out personally and thanks them for coming. Entering Cassalanter Villa, the party is a complication rather than an asset. Cassalanter response teams in Cassalanter Villa are briefed to watch them as unknown-hostile rather than unknown-neutral.

campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-05-the-cassalanter-dinner.md:100: The Cassalanters invited the party to dinner at their Sea Ward villa. Ammalia was warm, Victoro was precise, and the pitch was simple: tell them when you find the Stone. The party's response will define the Cassalanters' operational posture when Cassalanter Villa begins.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-05-the-cassalanter-dinner.md:104: Ammalia Cassalanter's dinner invitation brought the party to the villa on the evening after the fireball. Victoro acknowledged the "historical significance" of what Dalakhar was carrying and offered to share research in exchange for a first conversation when the Stone was found. The party's response determined whether the Cassalanters entered Cassalanter Villa as allies, neutrals, or adversaries.

campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-07-kalainsalley-tower.md:41: **Victoro Cassalanter's visit.** She will not volunteer this. If the party asks whether anyone else has come asking about Neverember's projects, she confirms: a man named Victoro Cassalanter visited a few weeks ago. He was asking many of the same questions Dalakhar had asked years before — the vault, Neverember's last projects, whether she kept his papers. He was polite. She found his politeness the most unsettling thing about the visit.

Through careful conversation, she confirmed Victoro Cassalanter had visited weeks before asking the same questions. Lord Dagult's lockbox in her tower held four documents, including the vault-opening method and an unsigned letter confirming Neverember had deliberately separated all three Eyes from the Stone four years ago.

campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m02-the-wazoo-affair/design-notes.md:5: The exposé comes from the source: a lurid piece on devil worship and orgies among unnamed Waterdhavian noble families, written by Jarlaxle and delivered by break-in. The restored draft made it accurate. It described a ritual space matching the Cassalanter villa's lower temple and credited a witness account to Nar'l Xibrindas, so Xanathar's Guild would have known the Cassalanters were infernalists before the party did. That contradicts the standing rule that no other faction knows.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m02-the-wazoo-affair/design-notes.md:7: The user ruled that Jarlaxle knows about the Cassalanters through a spy and won't tell members who haven't worked it out. The spy is Vessa, the Bregan D'aerthe doppelganger embedded in Cassalanter society, who reads thoughts and learned of the Asmodean pact and the children's contract that way. Here the piece stays rumor-shaped. It names no family and no witness. Its only particulars are an Aveen Street cellar and a Southern Ward windmill, given as footmen would give them, and it scatters hooks that fit several great houses: charity, a pleasant manner, a large fortune. Its purpose is pressure. Jarlaxle wants the Cassalanters to feel watched, and wants to see how they react and whom they send, without anyone tracing the piece to what he knows.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m02-the-wazoo-affair/ev-01-the-wazoo-affair.md:20: > - Jarlaxle knows the Cassalanters are bound to Asmodeus and have pledged their children in a contract. His doppelganger agent Vessa, who has moved in Cassalanter society for months, read it in their thoughts. No other faction or NPC in this Event knows.

They introduce themselves to Gaxly as solicitors acting for "a client who prefers to remain anonymous" and leave a card for "Harrow & Pell, Solicitors, Sea Ward". They ask who gave him the piece, warn him about the law of libel, and leave when it is clear he knows nothing.

campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m05-the-theaters-back-room/design-notes.md:7: His fear comes from **The Wazoo Affair**. The Cassalanters sent solicitors to a gossip sheet and then began questioning their own staff, so a steward who has carried baskets to the family's windmill reasonably believes he will be blamed. That link is the only one this mission needs from M2, and it holds with **Wazoo Exposé Published** and **Cassalanter Reaction Observed** as backstory. Nobody the party meets, except Bregan D'aerthe, needs to know about infernalism. Brimel knows about private gatherings, a hired cab with the curtains drawn, and Lord Victoro's word "resolution," which he takes for a debt. The restored line about Madam Ammalia visiting the week after her youngest was born is cut, because it points at the children's pact and the twins are eight.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m05-the-theaters-back-room/design-notes.md:11: The restored draft had Jarlaxle fishing for a secret the Wazoo piece had not yet turned up. Under the user's ruling, "Jarlaxle knows about the Cassalanters, he has a spy there. He won't tell the members unless they figure it out themselves," he already knows, through Vessa, the doppelganger embedded in the Cassalanter social circle. She reads thoughts, which is how he learned of the Asmodean pact and the children's contract. Vessa's cover reaches banquet halls, not a windmill on Coachlamp Lane. Brimel's plan fills that gap, and Krebbyg's delight at the Brandath map is real, because it is the company's first sight of where the family goes.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m05-the-theaters-back-room/design-notes.md:13: The speakers never confirm any of it to a member. The exception is the **Figuring It Out** rule: a member who says it in their own words, or shows proof, hears Zardoz or Nevercott admit the company has known for some time. A hunch does not unlock it, and the speaker does not name Vessa. **Cassalanter Pact Shared with BD** records the admission for **Cassalanter Villa** (unconverted), where Vessa helps that member openly, and for **Officer**, whose assessment may then state the truth.

campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m05-the-theaters-back-room/design-notes.md:30: Florette is now the 2024 **Spy** with four approaches, and a failure in any of them has a stated result: she notices a member's face, she follows Brimel at the interval, and the party faces two more checks before she can report. Three approaches (the double-booked seat, the lifted stub and the fire aisle) can leave her with nothing to report. The fourth, the service corridor, always ends in **Florette Reported**, because it is the choice to be seen. A party that takes it has not failed the mission and has paid a price that **Cassalanter Villa** will read. Captain Ilsa Dalloway and Constable Harl Pimm make force costly in a crowded theater. Pimm is the constable who counts lamp oil in **Kreb Drops the Cover**, so the party has met this Watch before.

campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m05-the-theaters-back-room/ev-01-the-theaters-back-room.md:18: > Brimel Crestfall is a household steward in the Cassalanter villa. For years he has carried baskets of candles, salt, black wax and wine to a converted windmill on Coachlamp Lane in the Southern Ward, as far as the second landing of its stair. The door at the top opens only to the two people who keep the rooms. The family pays the rent on that floor in the name of a Marra Selby and leaves the windmill out of the house books. Lord Victoro once told him it would be needed "for the resolution of the family's obligation," and Madam Ammalia goes out there alone twice a month in a hired cab with the curtains drawn. Brimel takes the "obligation" for a debt. He does not know what the family does there, and he does not know that the family is anything but rich, pious and generous. Nothing Brimel says gives the party more than suspicion.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m05-the-theaters-back-room/ev-01-the-theaters-back-room.md:20: > Brimel is not a man of conscience. Brimel believes the family will dismiss whoever it can blame. He took 300 gp from Bregan D'aerthe, through the household's wine merchant, to draw a floor plan of the windmill from memory. He has been paid 150 gp. The other 150 gp is in the envelope Krebbyg gives the member, and Brimel wants it to leave Waterdeep.

campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m05-the-theaters-back-room/overview.md:26: Brimel Crestfall is a household steward in the Cassalanter villa, a careful man in his forties. For years he has carried baskets of candles, salt, black wax and wine to a converted windmill on Coachlamp Lane in the Southern Ward. The family keeps the place off its social calendar and its house books and pays the rent on its upper floor in the name of a Marra Selby. Nobody goes there but Brimel and, twice a month, Madam Ammalia in a hired cab with the curtains drawn. Brimel does not know what the family does there. He knows that they hold private gatherings after dark, and that Lord Victoro once said the property would be needed "for the resolution of the family's obligation," which Brimel took to mean a debt.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m05-the-theaters-back-room/overview.md:58: Krebbyg reads the plan in the booking office after the show. A member who tells him in their own words that the Cassalanters are bound to a devil, or who shows proof, hears the company admit that it has known for some time, and **Cassalanter Pact Shared with BD** is marked.

campaign/quests/faction-events/order-of-the-gauntlet/m02-the-black-viper-investigation/design-notes.md:5: Appendix C gave the mission its spine. A Wazoo story says the Black Viper is Lady Ammalia's twin, a records check disproves it, the source was a well-dressed Tethyrian, and a small flying creature watches the party within two days. Savra's question asks who benefits from a lie that points investigators at a family. Everything else in the first draft was an addition, including the forged leaf, the red candle and the tea house.

campaign/quests/faction-events/order-of-the-gauntlet/m05-the-shrine-on-aveen-street/design-notes.md:5: Dragon Heist and Appendix C stop at the fifth Order mission, so this mission and the next are repo inventions. The restored draft gave a single locked door, one Bearded Devil and a ledger that explained a soul-bargain, twelve acts, a Reckoning and a Founders' Day deadline, and it moved Founders' Day four days earlier through a Masked Lord contact. Those pieces fail the Cassalanter secrecy rule and the calendar. Founders' Day is a fixed festival, and the pact belongs to **Cassalanter Villa**, not to a cellar in the Field Ward.

The mission awards a Siamorphe holy symbol at its end so that **Cassalanter Villa** can read it from the party's possessions, and the chaplain says she does not know what it does. The quiet route is a swap with a stock ledger from Orros's shop, which gives the Bearded Devil a non-combat answer without letting it leave the ring.

The Harper can pass the words on, and any Order member who hears them can carry them to Savra.

campaign/setting/deities/asmodeus.md:65: > - **What the pact does not make true:** He has no Chosen in the campaign and takes no part in any scene. campaign/setting/deities/asmodeus.md:75: > - **Who is tied to him:** The Cassalanters. Victoro Cassalanter is a half-elf banker and secret priest of Asmodeus. Ammalia Cassalanter is a human wizard and secret worshipper. Tissina Khyret, Laiba "Nana" Rosse, Jandar Chergoba, Seffia Naelryke and Arn Xalrondar are cult fanatics. Willifort Crowelle, the doppelganger butler, is their trusted operative.
campaign/setting/deities/asmodeus.md:76: > - **The pact:** Three years before the campaign, about 1489 DR, the family had held its noble name for several generations and was facing financial ruin. Victoro and Ammalia chose to trade their three children's souls to Asmodeus for wealth compressed from three generations into one and political access that normally takes centuries to build. The deal also bought power, good health and long life. They understood the terms and signed. Financial ruin was the trigger. (see **A Debt in Blood**)
campaign/setting/deities/asmodeus.md:78: > - **The deadline:** The pact comes due on Flamerule 11, the twins' ninth birthday. Founders' Day, Flamerule 1, is the feast where the family plans to collect ninety-nine souls, and it is the party's real deadline. Ammalia chose that date as her operational window, for the civic cover and for the time left to verify the terms, and it does not appear in the contract.
campaign/setting/deities/asmodeus.md:81: > - **The temple:** The family keeps an illegal temple to Asmodeus beneath Cassalanter Villa. Its discovery is a capital Code Legal offense. Worship alone is not outlawed (see Influence and Followers), so the capital charge rests on the temple and what is done in it.
campaign/setting/deities/asmodeus.md:82: > - **Cover:** Victoro is publicly a worshipper of Lathander, and the family is publicly known for its ancestral devotion to Siamorphe. The family is known for charity, and none of that warmth is faked.
campaign/setting/deities/asmodeus.md:83: > - **Who knows (secrecy rule):** Nobody, before the party finds out. Every faction is suspicious of the Cassalanters because they are too nice to be this rich and this purebred, and none knows about the pact, the soul contract or the infernalism. The one exception is Jarlaxle, who knows through Vessa, his doppelganger spy in the Cassalanter social circle. He tells Bregan D'aerthe members nothing unless they work it out. The Emerald Enclave senses infernal influence in the Sea Ward through Jeryth and does not know whose it is. Savra Belabranta of the Order of the Gauntlet hunts infernal cults in Waterdeep and wants to learn what the Cassalanters conceal, which is suspicion only.
campaign/setting/deities/asmodeus.md:84: > - **Use at the table:** Players who ask a sage or a priest about Asmodeus get the Overview. Players who ask about devils and pacts in the context of the family get nothing until the party's own evidence supports it. Resolutions of the pact itself are written in **Cassalanter Villa**, not here.

campaign/setting/deities/lathander.md:66: > - **The ordinary Waterdhavian's view:** Voice him as the god of fresh starts. A sage, a priest or a regular at the bar says he is the Morninglord, the god of dawn, birth and renewal, and that you pray to him before a journey, a wedding or a new business. They add that his priests dress in rose, orange and gold, that his temples are rich and a little gaudy, and that his faithful have no use for undead. The temple they name is the Spires of the Morning in the Castle Ward. On Rhyestertide, the first of Ches, they say the faithful cover one eye until the next dawn, and a newcomer who catches a celebrant's eye should wink. Most Waterdhavians know Victoro Cassalanter, the banker and philanthropist, as a worshiper of his.

campaign/setting/deities/lathander.md:87: Lathander, the Morninglord, is the god of dawn, birth and renewal. People across Faerûn pray to him before journeys, weddings and new ventures. His temple in Waterdeep is the Spires of the Morning in the Castle Ward, one of the city's most beautiful. His priests dress in the rose, orange and gold of sunrise. On Rhyestertide, the first of Ches, the faithful wear the same colors and cover one eye until the next dawn. Most Waterdhavians know the banker and philanthropist Victoro Cassalanter as one of his worshipers.
campaign/setting/deities/lathander.md:100: > - **What the cover hides:** Victoro is a priest of Asmodeus, and his devotion to the Lord of the Nine Hells is a secret known only to his wife and his closest friends (*Dragon Heist*). Most Waterdhavians know him as a banker, philanthropist and worshiper of Lathander. The pact behind it is set out on the **Asmodeus** page.
campaign/setting/deities/lathander.md:101: > - **The cover is deliberate:** The campaign gives Victoro Lathander and the house Siamorphe as public cover, which matches *Dragon Heist*. The philanthropist's reputation is a working part of the plan, and the family's charity is real. See **Siamorphe** for the other half of the cover.

campaign/setting/deities/siamorphe.md:51: > - **Campaign ties:** The Cassalanters are "publicly known for its ancestral devotion to Siamorphe" (the Cassalanters organization page, Overview). **Cassalanter Villa** uses her statue, her sun symbols, her holy symbol and a mace consecrated to her. campaign/setting/deities/siamorphe.md:61: > - **The temple was hers:** The temple beneath Cassalanter Villa was a temple of Siamorphe until Victoro and Ammalia's devil-worship desecrated it, and its great chapel is now dedicated to Asmodeus (*Dragon Heist*).
campaign/setting/deities/siamorphe.md:64: > - **Caladorn Cassalanter:** The ghost of Victoro's father was a Masked Lord and hero of the North who devoted his life to Siamorphe. He is bound in the family crypt, off the temple's anterior vestibule, with three specters. His plaque reads "Servant of Siamorphe in his time," and he knows the statue was his wife's image of the goddess before the family defaced it.
campaign/setting/deities/siamorphe.md:72: > - **Where the party gets the symbol:** At the end of **The Shrine on Aveen Street**, Chaplain Ysolt Brannock gives one consecrated symbol to the party, and the members choose who carries it. It is a brass sun with eight spokes on a leather cord. **Cassalanter Villa** reads it from the party's possessions.
campaign/setting/deities/siamorphe.md:73: > - **The fallback:** If that mission has not run, a Whitehawk member gets one from Savra Belabranta once the party holds evidence from **Faction Outposts** or has marked **Cassalanter Register Filed**. A party that took nothing from the Aveen Street cellar gets no symbol from the mission, and Whitehawk is its other source.
campaign/setting/deities/siamorphe.md:75: > - **Signs of her that remain above the temple:** The ballroom floor is a gold and white tile mosaic of her sun symbol, five feet across and unchanged. The wine cellar's west wall carries a sun with eight spokes in relief, which marks the secret door. Elzerina Cassalanter knows only "a funny door behind a sun." The Stone's vision at **Sea Maidens Faire** shows a Sea Ward villa with a sun symbol marking a hidden door, and it does not name her. The twelve sarcophagi in the crypt bear the family crest, a sun over a scale in her heraldic tradition.
campaign/setting/deities/siamorphe.md:77: > - **Striking the statue:** The mace reacts at once, because the consecration still lives in the stone. The Asmodeus features crack away over about six seconds and show fragments of Siamorphe's original face beneath. The four resolutions of the pact are written in **Cassalanter Villa**, not here.

campaign/setting/deities/waukeen.md:86: > - **Waukeentide and Cassalanter Villa:** The Cassalanters host three events in the tenday, and the Cassalanter Villa structure document uses all three as windows. Caravance (Tarsakh 1) is the holiday on which the family gives most of its staff the night off. Goldenight (Tarsakh 5) is a gala for 40 to 60 guests. The Waukeentide Charity Ball (Tarsakh 7) is a fundraiser for Field Ward reconstruction and the main social entry point. The festival calendar lists the same three dates as the villa's entry points. Staffing, rosters and the checks are in **Cassalanter Villa**, not here.

campaign/setting/faction-operations/10-cassalanters.md:21: If the party agreed to help, the Cassalanters go quiet. With the characters in the field as their agents, drawing attention to themselves risks unraveling the arrangement. The Cassalanters may dispatch Seffia's team if the party requests backup or if they need to insert themselves without appearing to act. Willifort keeps tabs on the characters from a distance, reporting to Ammalia, and the family's response apparatus holds.
campaign/setting/faction-operations/10-cassalanters.md:27: Two teams carry out Cassalanter field operations. Seffia's team is precision work; Willifort's is a blunt instrument the family draws only once discretion has failed.
campaign/setting/faction-operations/10-cassalanters.md:47: *Area 6 — Mirror of the Fiend.* An obsidian-framed mirror that deals 1d6 damage per round to most creatures and heals 1d6 per round for fiends. A carved Cassalanter noble sigil marks the back. Any character who examines the mirror and makes a successful DC 13 Intelligence (History) check recognizes it as a Waterdhavian noble marking; on a result of 16 or higher, the character identifies the family.
campaign/setting/faction-operations/10-cassalanters.md:55: The apprentice wizard in Area 2 carries *Instructions from Lord Cassalanter*, directing that the next pair of summoned Imps be sent to surveil the Sea Maidens Faire.
campaign/setting/faction-operations/10-cassalanters.md:76: **Escalation:** If the Asmodean Shrine has already been struck, the Cassalanters move to Lockdown (Tier 4); if this is the first Cassalanter outpost hit, they move to Alert (Tier 3). If the sentry Imp escapes, the Cassalanters know exactly what was taken. **Founders' Day Clock (Flamerule 1): 3–5 days.**

campaign/setting/governance-and-guilds/city-watch.md:66: **Jalester Silvermane** (Lawful Good, Cormyrean Human, he/him) is a Lords' Alliance field agent who reports to Laeral and carries a badge of the Watch. He reads the Watch's file ledger each tenday, and his briefings in the **Cassalanter Villa** and **Kolat Towers** structure documents cover Watch patrol schedules and Watch coverage.
campaign/setting/governance-and-guilds/city-watch.md:84: > - **Who the Watch cannot reach:** In the **Cassalanter Villa** structure document, Jalester's briefing says the Watch cannot touch the Cassalanters without evidence because they are too well connected. Mirt suspects, without proof, that Cassalanter money stood behind the Howling Hatred cult three years ago, since the Watch released two of its members after a word from someone at Cassalanter Villa. The Cassalanters' first response to the party is leverage, and a threat of Watch involvement is part of it. Willifort Crowelle may also arrange for a Cassalanter-connected Watch lieutenant to take a new interest in the party's tavern.
campaign/setting/governance-and-guilds/code-legal-and-magisters.md:60: > - **The temple offense:** In the campaign, an illegal temple to Asmodeus lies beneath **Cassalanter Villa**, and discovering it is a capital offense under the Code Legal. Worship alone is not outlawed, so the capital charge rests on the temple and on what is done in it. This is campaign-only, and no published source names a temple or worship offense. Nobody outside the household and the cult knows about the temple, so no NPC cites this offense against the Cassalanters until the party has found the temple. See **The Cassalanters**.

A character who makes a DC 12 Intelligence (History) check on them learns they amount to control of the Chandlers', Coopers' and Carpenters' guilds, among others (campaign-only). campaign/setting/history/05-a-debt-in-blood.md:17: **Span:** 1489 DR to 1492 DR, three years. The published adventure says only "three years ago" (lines 18294 and 18403). The year 1489 DR is that phrase counted back from 1492 DR. The Forgotten Realms Wiki states 1489 DR for the pact on its Cassalanter, Victoro, Ammalia and Osvaldo pages, and those figures derive from the same adventure line. The Alexandrian timeline dates Neverember's deposition to 1489 DR and gives no pact year. The campaign opens on Ches 1, 1492 DR.
campaign/setting/history/05-a-debt-in-blood.md:19: **Other Names:** The Cassalanter Bargain; the Soul Pact. These are GM names taken from the old era block and the guides. No in-world name is sourced. `[research]`
campaign/setting/history/05-a-debt-in-blood.md:24: > - Victoro and Ammalia Cassalanter, of a house with several generations in Waterdeep's noble registry, traded the souls of their three children to Asmodeus for wealth and political access. They read the terms before they signed.
campaign/setting/history/05-a-debt-in-blood.md:27: > - Their recovery plan needs 999,999 gp and ninety-nine souls, both, to reclaim the claims on the twins. The souls are the ninety-nine guests poisoned at the Founders' Day feast. The family holds about 500,000 gp of its own, and the Vault's 500,000 gp is the shortfall. Founders' Day (Flamerule 1) is the feast and Ammalia's real deadline, her operational window. The contract falls due on Flamerule 11 and does not name Founders' Day.
campaign/setting/history/05-a-debt-in-blood.md:33: - The Cassalanter name has stood in Waterdeep's noble registry for several generations. The published adventure shows several generations of family portraits.
campaign/setting/history/05-a-debt-in-blood.md:34: - Caladorn Cassalanter was Victoro's father, a Masked Lord and a hero of the North (`adventure-wdh.json` lines 18404 and 22713). His ghost haunts the family crypt beneath the villa. The wiki also lists him as the fifth Open Lord, which the published adventure and the campaign do not (Forgotten Realms Wiki, Lords of Waterdeep and Caladorn Cassalanter; City of Splendors: Waterdeep, 2e). See **A City Watching Itself**.
campaign/setting/history/05-a-debt-in-blood.md:37: - Victoro and Ammalia discussed the bargain, understood what they were signing and signed together.
campaign/setting/history/05-a-debt-in-blood.md:39: - The contract reached them through a pit fiend intermediary, and they signed in the temple beneath their own villa. Source: the **Cassalanter Villa** quest opener.
campaign/setting/history/05-a-debt-in-blood.md:44: - The contract claims each child at nine. Osvaldo was already nine when his parents signed, so he was taken at once. The twins' souls are forfeit on their ninth birthday, Flamerule 11. The old block gives no birthday. Source: the **Cassalanter Villa** quest opener and Ammalia's grimoire in the villa's study.
campaign/setting/history/05-a-debt-in-blood.md:52: - Osvaldo is a chain devil held in the soundproofed attic of the villa, room C24, never the basement or the temple. Ammalia visits weekly and sits in the room outside the pentagram. Victoro has not entered in months. Willifort knows. Two imps are always present.
campaign/setting/history/05-a-debt-in-blood.md:59: - Ammalia's charity in the Field Ward feeds hundreds, and Victoro's banking underwrites half the guilds in the city. Source: the **Cassalanter Villa** quest opener.
campaign/setting/history/05-a-debt-in-blood.md:60: - The family's generosity funds temples, orphanages and civic improvements. Ammalia keeps the most beautiful butterfly garden in Waterdeep and supervises the twins at play there.
campaign/setting/history/05-a-debt-in-blood.md:64: > *I cannot claim to know the Cassalanters well, having dined at their villa only twice, but on both evenings Lady Ammalia led me through her butterfly garden before the soup, naming every specimen and the country it came from. Arrive prepared to be educated (and to be hungry). She corrected my pronunciation of three of the names so gently that I wrote them down afterward, meaning to use them at my next dinner.*
campaign/setting/history/05-a-debt-in-blood.md:72: - Founders' Day, Flamerule 1, is the feast and Ammalia's real deadline. It is her operational window, chosen for the civic cover and for the time left to verify the terms. The contract falls due on Flamerule 11, the twins' ninth birthday, and does not name Founders' Day.
campaign/setting/history/05-a-debt-in-blood.md:77: - The irreversible act is Ammalia's feast. On Founders' Day she will invite one hundred of Waterdeep's poorest to a charitable dinner at the villa and poison ninety-nine of them, so that the guests die the next day and nothing connects them to the house.
campaign/setting/history/05-a-debt-in-blood.md:78: - The family's warmth toward the twins is real. Victoro and Ammalia carry the decision together, Ammalia makes the final calls, and Victoro has stopped questioning them.
campaign/setting/history/05-a-debt-in-blood.md:82: The Cassalanters are one of Waterdeep's old noble houses, with several generations in the city's registry and a name now tied to open-handed charity. Lord Victoro, a half-elf banker, and Lady Ammalia, a human wizard, fund temples, orphanages and civic improvements across the city, and Victoro's banking underwrites half the guilds. Ammalia's butterfly garden is counted the most beautiful in Waterdeep, and their young twins play there under her eye. Their eldest son is said to be studying in a faraway city, and the family does not often speak of him.

campaign/setting/history/09-timekeeping-and-the-festival-calendar.md:91: - **The feast.** Ammalia Cassalanter invites one hundred of Waterdeep's poorest to a charity dinner on the villa's lawn pavilions through her Field Ward charity. The guests are served ninety-nine cups of wine with midnight tears, and they die within 24 hours, scattered across the city. In the campaign the feast begins at noon.
campaign/setting/history/09-timekeeping-and-the-festival-calendar.md:92: - **Why that date.** The civic holiday makes an invitation to the poor unremarkable, Ammalia's charity makes the feast plausible, and ten days before the birthday leaves time to verify that the contract's terms are met.
