campaign/setting/notable-figures/bregan-daerthe/01-jarlaxle-baenre.md:8: > - **Featured in:** **Finding Floon**, **Trollskull Alley**, **Fireball!**, **Gralhund Villa**, **Faction Outposts**, **Xanathar's Lair**, **Cassalanter Villa**, **Sea Maidens Faire**, **Kolat Towers**, **Vault of Dragons**, **The Handkerchief and the Girl**, **The Wazoo Affair**, **The Betrayal Pitch**, **Three Nights**, **The Compromised Eye**, **The Theater's Back Room**, **The Dive**, **Doom Raiders Mission 1 — The Dockside Killer**, **Force Grey Mission 2 — The Dragon in the Harbor**, **Force Grey Mission 4 — Destroy the Intellect Factory**, **A Friend's House**
campaign/setting/notable-figures/bregan-daerthe/01-jarlaxle-baenre.md:26: **Relationships.** Jarlaxle is the leader of Bregan D'Aerthe, the secret lord of Luskan, the employer of Soluun, Fel'rekt, and Krebbyg, and a rival who may become an ally if the party offers him something worth more than the gold. He has known of the Stone, the Eyes, and the vault since reaching Waterdeep, no later than Marpenoth 3, 1491 DR. He alone outside the household and the cult knows of the Cassalanter pact, through his doppelganger spy Vessa. Eye #3 is aboard the *Scarlet Marpenoth*.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/bregan-daerthe/12-vessa.md:8: > - **Featured in:** **Cassalanter Villa**, **Sea Maidens Faire**
campaign/setting/notable-figures/bregan-daerthe/12-vessa.md:16: **Motivations.** Vessa wants to maintain her cover in the Cassalanter social scene, observe the outcome of the **Cassalanter Villa** operation, and report accurate intelligence to Jarlaxle. She does not want to enter the temple.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/bregan-daerthe/12-vessa.md:22: **Persona.** To the world, "Vessa" is a noblewoman circulating through the Cassalanter banquet hall with the ease of someone who has been attending these events for months, which she has. To BD-affiliated party members who approach her, she provides a brief unambiguous signal — a flash of gray features, a knowing look — before returning to the social performance; to everyone else, she is a mysterious woman who seems to know too much. Deep down, she has been doing this long enough that she has to think before remembering what her own face looks like, and has decided this is probably fine.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/bregan-daerthe/12-vessa.md:26: **Relationships.** Vessa (full name Lymeria Lhaurilstar) is a Bregan D'aerthe doppelganger operative embedded in the Cassalanter social scene under a permanent human cover, Jarlaxle's primary intelligence source on the Cassalanter operation throughout **Cassalanter Villa**, a useful ally to BD-affiliated party members (distraction, cover stories, social-exposure suppression), and the source of the intelligence adjustment Jarlaxle makes to his Eye #3 strategy once **Cassalanter Villa** resolves.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/01-victoro-cassalanter.md:1: # Victoro Cassalanter
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/01-victoro-cassalanter.md:9: > - **Featured in:** Fireball!, Faction Outposts, Xanathar's Lair, Cassalanter Villa, Vault of Dragons, The Wazoo Affair, The Theater's Back Room, Spined Devils in Twelvedog Court, The Shrine on Aveen Street, The Writ and the Weight, Cassalanter Villa (location)
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/01-victoro-cassalanter.md:13: **Resonance.** Victoro should inspire unease with his perfect manners (which never falter even while planning mass murder), sympathy when the party learns about the pact and the children, and horror when they realize the sympathy was calculated.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/01-victoro-cassalanter.md:15: **Emotions.** Victoro most often feels calculated charm, paternal desperation masked by composure, cold theological certainty, genuine love for his children twisted by his own choices, strategic patience, and a self-serving hunger for power that he mistakes for devotion.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/01-victoro-cassalanter.md:17: **Motivations.** Victoro wants to save his younger twins from the Asmodean pact before their ninth birthday, acquire 999,999 gold pieces to fund the buy-out clause, and sacrifice 99 souls at the Founders' Day feast while maintaining his philanthropist reputation.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/01-victoro-cassalanter.md:19: **Inspirations.** When playing Victoro, channel Tywin Lannister (*Game of Thrones*), Frollo (*The Hunchback of Notre Dame*), and Gustavo Fring (*Breaking Bad*).
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/01-victoro-cassalanter.md:23: **Persona.** To the world, Victoro is a devilishly handsome half-elf banker, philanthropist, and worshipper of Lathander whose profits fund charity, with an unreadable smile and a genuine warmth toward his younger children that makes the rest of what he is harder to see. To those he trusts, he is a secret priest of Asmodeus whose pact traded his three children's souls for power. Deep down, everything the Cassalanters tell the PCs about their situation is technically true; they do not mention that the ritual also requires poisoning 99 people at the Founders' Day feast. He has not entered the attic where Osvaldo is in months and cannot bring himself to go; that vigil belongs to Ammalia. He carries a ruby-tipped cane that functions as a rod of rulership.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/01-victoro-cassalanter.md:25: **Morale.** In a fight, Victoro uses his priestly magic and social authority to de-escalate, negotiate, or bribe. If combat is unavoidable, he fights to protect his family and his secret, and he is slow to anger but devastating when pushed.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/01-victoro-cassalanter.md:27: **Relationships.** Victoro is the husband of Ammalia Cassalanter (co-conspirator), the father of Osvaldo (eldest, soul lost, transformed into a chain devil), Terenzio and Elzerina (the twins, forfeit on their ninth birthday), and the employer of Willifort Crowelle (doppelganger butler who knows everything that happens in the villa).
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/01-victoro-cassalanter.md:31: Victoro Cassalanter is a devilishly handsome half-elf banker and philanthropist. His profits are said to fund charities across Waterdeep, and his easy warmth with his younger children is as well-known as his unreadable smile.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/02-ammalia-cassalanter.md:1: # Ammalia Cassalanter
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/02-ammalia-cassalanter.md:9: > - **Featured in:** Trollskull Alley, Fireball!, Cassalanter Villa, Vault of Dragons, The Theater's Back Room, Cassalanter Villa (location)
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/02-ammalia-cassalanter.md:13: **Resonance.** Ammalia should inspire wariness with her exquisite manners and probing intelligence, sympathy when the party sees her with the twins in the butterfly garden, and cold dread when they learn she is the one planning the poisoned feast.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/02-ammalia-cassalanter.md:15: **Emotions.** Ammalia most often feels shrewd calculation, maternal protectiveness twisted by desperation, vainglorious self-assurance, careful control over every social interaction, intellectual pleasure in negotiation, and a private grief about Osvaldo that she channels into ruthless action.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/02-ammalia-cassalanter.md:17: **Motivations.** Ammalia wants to save the twins by any means necessary (including poisoning 99 people), maintain the family's social position, and ensure that the Founders' Day feast goes exactly as planned.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/02-ammalia-cassalanter.md:19: **Inspirations.** When playing Ammalia, channel Lady Macbeth (*Macbeth*), Mama Imelda (*Coco*), and Catherine de Medici (*The Serpent Queen*).
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/02-ammalia-cassalanter.md:23: **Persona.** To the world, Ammalia is well-mannered, well-read, well-traveled, and known for driving a hard bargain. Her butterfly garden is the most beautiful in Waterdeep, and she supervises her youngest children at play there. To those she trusts, she worships Asmodeus alongside Victoro and shares the same pact and the same desperation. Deep down, while Victoro hunts Neverember's gold, Ammalia makes the plans: the feast, the poison, the 99 souls. She also goes to the attic where Osvaldo is bound, and reads him his favorite stories from a chair placed just outside the shielding circle while he curses her name and pleads with her to save him.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/02-ammalia-cassalanter.md:25: **Morale.** In a fight, Ammalia grabs the twins and runs. She will spend Victoro's life to buy that retreat without hesitating, and her arcane magic is precise enough to cover an exit while collapsing a hallway behind her.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/02-ammalia-cassalanter.md:27: **Relationships.** Ammalia is the wife of Victoro Cassalanter, the mother of Osvaldo, Terenzio, and Elzerina, and the operational architect of the Founders' Day feast. Her lepidopterology hobby gives her an excuse to spend hours in the garden with her children, which is both genuine and calculated.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/02-ammalia-cassalanter.md:31: Ammalia Cassalanter is a noblewoman known for driving a hard bargain and for keeping the most beautiful butterfly garden in Waterdeep, where she can often be found supervising her youngest children at play.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/03-willifort-crowelle.md:8: > - **Featured in:** Faction Outposts, Cassalanter Villa, Vault of Dragons, Cassalanter Villa (location)
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/03-willifort-crowelle.md:16: **Motivations.** Willifort wants to protect the Cassalanter family and their secrets, maintain his cover as an aging tiefling butler, and handle problems permanently when discretion fails.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/03-willifort-crowelle.md:26: **Relationships.** Willifort is the chief butler and Lord Victoro's personal attendant, a doppelganger who holds keys to every room alongside Victoro and Ammalia, and the Cassalanters' intelligence operative who reports to Ammalia on everything that transpires within the villa.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/03-willifort-crowelle.md:30: Willifort Crowelle is the Cassalanter family's chief butler — an aging tiefling in a sharp black suit and thin-rimmed spectacles who greets every guest with impeccable courtesy and seems to know exactly what is needed before anyone asks.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/04-osvaldo-cassalanter.md:1: # Osvaldo Cassalanter
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/04-osvaldo-cassalanter.md:8: > - **Featured in:** Cassalanter Villa, Cassalanter Villa (location)
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/04-osvaldo-cassalanter.md:14: **Emotions.** Osvaldo most often feels the chain devil's rage against confinement, fragmented grief for a life that ended at nine, desperate pleading when Ammalia visits, a hatred of his own condition that sometimes expresses as hatred of everything, and the specific anguish of knowing his siblings are scheduled for the same fate.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/04-osvaldo-cassalanter.md:22: **Persona.** To the world, Osvaldo Cassalanter is a name the family does not mention — "attending school in a faraway city," Terenzio and Elzerina were told. To those who open the attic door, he is a chain devil in a soundproofed attic who attacks immediately, and whose chains have a 15-foot reach. Deep down, some fragment of Osvaldo is still there, and it hears his mother's voice when she comes to read, and it is very much aware of what was done to it.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/04-osvaldo-cassalanter.md:24: **Morale.** Osvaldo attacks anything that enters the attic except Ammalia. Her presence calms him enough for her to read from outside the shielding circle, but she does not go into the room.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/04-osvaldo-cassalanter.md:26: **Relationships.** Osvaldo is the eldest child of Victoro and Ammalia (taken at nine, twelve now, his soul surrendered to Asmodeus three years ago during the family's financial crisis), the reason Terenzio and Elzerina are forfeit on their ninth birthday, Flamerule 11, 1492 DR (the pact requires both 999,999 gp and ninety-nine souls), and the tragedy that both explains and indicts everything his parents are doing.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/04-osvaldo-cassalanter.md:30: Osvaldo Cassalanter is the family's eldest son — absent from public life, said to be attending school in a faraway city, and not a subject his parents discuss with guests.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/05-terenzio-cassalanter.md:1: # Terenzio Cassalanter
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/05-terenzio-cassalanter.md:8: > - **Featured in:** Cassalanter Villa, Cassalanter Villa (location)
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/05-terenzio-cassalanter.md:30: Terenzio Cassalanter is one of the family's young twins, currently best known for wearing a dragon costume around the villa while being chased by his sister with a wooden sword.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/06-elzerina-cassalanter.md:1: # Elzerina Cassalanter
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/06-elzerina-cassalanter.md:8: > - **Featured in:** Cassalanter Villa, Cassalanter Villa (location)
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/06-elzerina-cassalanter.md:30: Elzerina Cassalanter is Terenzio's twin and the bolder of the two — a child in a knight's helm currently chasing her brother around the villa with a wooden sword, who asks visitors flat-out whether they fight monsters.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/07-tissina-khyret.md:8: > - **Featured in:** Cassalanter Villa
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/07-tissina-khyret.md:14: **Emotions.** Tissina most often feels attentive devotion to Ammalia, theological certainty about Asmodeus, professional alertness to threats to the household, and the particular steadiness of someone who has never doubted the choice she made.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/07-tissina-khyret.md:16: **Motivations.** Tissina wants to protect Ammalia and the household, serve Asmodeus through that service, and identify threats to the villa before they become problems.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/07-tissina-khyret.md:22: **Persona.** To the world, Tissina Khyret is the tiefling attendant who stands near Ammalia at social functions and anticipates what is needed before it is asked. To those she considers threats, she is the first person in the villa to notice something is wrong. Deep down, her worship of Ammalia and her worship of Asmodeus flow from the same devotion, and she has never seen any contradiction between them.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/07-tissina-khyret.md:24: **Morale.** In a fight, Tissina protects Ammalia first, using cult fanatic spells to hold threats at distance while Ammalia escapes.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/07-tissina-khyret.md:26: **Relationships.** Tissina is Ammalia Cassalanter's personal tiefling attendant, a cult fanatic whose regalia is stored alongside Willifort Crowelle's in a shared lockbox, and the first cultist in the villa to notice an infiltration.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/07-tissina-khyret.md:30: Tissina Khyret is Ammalia Cassalanter's personal tiefling attendant, a composed and courteous woman who stands near her mistress at social functions and seems to anticipate needs before they are expressed.

campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/09-jandar-chergoba.md:8: > - **Featured in:** Cassalanter Villa, Cassalanter Villa (location)

To those who threaten the kitchen, a single word raises every knife in the room. Deep down, the Founders' Day feast is the most important project of his career, and he has approached it with the same precision he brings to everything else.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/09-jandar-chergoba.md:26: **Relationships.** Jandar is the Cassalanter Villa's head chef, a cult fanatic, the keeper of golden sacrificial implements hidden under a sack of potatoes, and the person who determines whether the Founders' Day poison is distributed correctly.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/09-jandar-chergoba.md:30: Jandar Chergoba is the Cassalanter Villa's head chef, a tiefling who runs his kitchen with the efficiency of a general and produces food that guests remember long after they leave.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/10-seffia-naelryke.md:10: > - **Featured in:** Faction Outposts, Cassalanter Villa, Cassalanter Villa (location)
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/10-seffia-naelryke.md:19: - She leads the Cassalanter response team from the Converted Windmill on Coachlamp Lane in the Southern Ward. The team is Seffia, 2 Spies, 1 Veteran and 1 invisible Imp, and it does precision work: intelligence gathering, surveillance and combat support.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/10-seffia-naelryke.md:24: - She keeps a letter from Lord Cassalanter confirming that her ritual identified Eye #2 in the Brandath Crypt. It is a prized possession, and anyone who takes it provokes her fury.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/10-seffia-naelryke.md:25: - She reports in writing on the Cassalanters' external outposts, covering the Converted Windmill and the Asmodean Shrine. Her report sits in the hidden compartment of Victoro Cassalanter's desk at the villa.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/10-seffia-naelryke.md:27: - Under escalation she acts in two modes. At Alert her team stays passive unless the party turns hostile, and then she and Willifort Crowelle work together. At Lockdown during **Cassalanter Villa** her team is stationed inside the villa.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/10-seffia-naelryke.md:34: - **Victoro Cassalanter:** The patron who sent her to retrieve an Eye (Eye #2, from the Brandath Crypt) and to silence three cult members who knew too much. Unresolved: she means to repair the failure without telling him.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/10-seffia-naelryke.md:35: - **Vaelle Lurval:** The witness she failed to silence, a Lawful Evil female Tethyrian human cultist and a 30-year-old Waterdhavian florist. The two dead cultists are Holiver Tornrudder, her boyfriend, and Kaeth Warloon. Vaelle believes the fanatics acted on their own, not on Lord Cassalanter's orders, and she will not name the Cassalanters unless magically compelled. Unresolved: Vaelle is alive, and Seffia is working on the problem. Vaelle has no Notable Figures page and appears nowhere else in the campaign files.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/10-seffia-naelryke.md:53: **Motivations.** Seffia wants to recover from the Vaelle Lurval failure without exposing it to Lord Cassalanter, resume her response team role, and maintain the Converted Windmill as a reliable Asmodean fallback location.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/10-seffia-naelryke.md:63: **Relationships.** Seffia is the Cassalanter response team leader (stationed at the Converted Windmill, Southern Ward), Arn Xalrondar's operational partner, the cult fanatic who failed to silence Vaelle Lurval, and a recurring NPC across **Faction Outposts** and **Cassalanter Villa**.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/10-seffia-naelryke.md:68: > Seffia is an Asmodean cult fanatic in the Cassalanters' service, a fact the party learns only by investigating the Windmill. Lord Cassalanter gave her two missions, to retrieve Eye #2 of the Stone of Golorr from the Brandath Crypt and to silence three cult members who knew too much about it. She and Arn killed two of the three. The survivor, Vaelle Lurval, is the loose end Seffia is working to close, and Lord Cassalanter does not know she failed.

Lines 8317-8319 give Vaelle as a Lawful Evil female Tethyrian human cultist and florist, with Holiver Tornrudder and Kaeth Warloon as the dead.

campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/11-arn-xalrondar.md:8: > - **Featured in:** Faction Outposts, Cassalanter Villa
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/11-arn-xalrondar.md:22: **Persona.** To the world, Arn Xalrondar sleeps at the Windmill with Seffia and defers to her judgment on everything that involves planning. To those who investigate, his Book of Worship contains the Windmill's address as the place of assembly, and the handprints in the apartment match those at Cassalanter Villa. Deep down, he made the right call giving the Stone to the spined devil — the mission required it, the Stone escaped, and he will not be talked out of believing the decision was correct.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/11-arn-xalrondar.md:26: **Relationships.** Arn is the Cassalanter Converted Windmill co-occupant, Seffia Naelryke's operational partner, the cultist whose Book of Worship links directly to the Windmill address (an intelligence seed for any investigation), and the man who sent the Stone of Golorr out with a spined devil rather than allow the party to take it.

campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/13-ivrala-sorn.md:6: > - *Human spy (Cassalanter operative), neutral evil*. Stat block: **Spy**.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/13-ivrala-sorn.md:22: **Persona.** To the world, Ivrala Sorn is a reliable, professional server who arrives on time, keeps orders correct, and remembers patron preferences. To those who know what they are looking at, she is filing weekly reports on Trollskull Manor's regulars, their faction affiliations, and who is drinking with whom. Deep down, Sister Valdra has seen her face in Cassalanter Villa's staff quarters, and she recognizes it.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/13-ivrala-sorn.md:26: **Relationships.** Ivrala is a Cassalanter intelligence operative placed as a Trollskull Manor server candidate, a person reporting to Willifort Crowelle on patron activity, recognizable to Sister Valdra on a DC 13 Perception check when both are present, and a staff candidate in the **Trollskull Manor** guide.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/14-caladorn-cassalanter.md:1: # Caladorn Cassalanter
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/14-caladorn-cassalanter.md:6: > - *Ghost of a Cassalanter noble, lawful good*. Stat block: **Ghost**.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/14-caladorn-cassalanter.md:8: > - **Featured in:** Cassalanter Villa, Cassalanter Villa (location)
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/14-caladorn-cassalanter.md:16: **Motivations.** Caladorn wants to see the infernal corruption of his house ended, the effigy in A7 destroyed, and some portion of the honor his family carried for generations restored to the name he left behind. He does not want revenge on Victoro and Ammalia. He wants the bargain broken.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/14-caladorn-cassalanter.md:22: **Persona.** To the world — specifically to anyone who enters A4a bearing a Siamorphe holy symbol — Caladorn is an armored figure with silver hair and the Cassalanter features, carrying the family sword, voice steady until it is not. His portrait hangs prominently in the entrance hall upstairs, silver-haired and armored; the family uses his image as civic virtue while remaining unable to reach him. To those who present the symbol and wait, he is lucid, measured, and specific about what he is asking and what he is giving in return. Deep down, they turned his wife's goddess into that thing, and he has been twelve feet away from it for three years.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/14-caladorn-cassalanter.md:26: **Relationships.** Caladorn is the ghost of Victoro Cassalanter's father, a former Masked Lord and hero of the North who devoted his life to Siamorphe, the former owner of two items critical to the contract-destruction path (the +1 plate armor and the mace of disruption), and the reason the solution to the Cassalanter pact has been beneath the villa the entire time — accessible only to people not corrupted by it.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/14-caladorn-cassalanter.md:30: Caladorn Cassalanter's portrait hangs prominently in the villa's entrance hall — silver-haired and armored, a former Masked Lord and hero of the North whose image the family still invokes as civic virtue.

campaign/setting/notable-figures/gralhunds/04-hurv-taldred.md:12: **Resonance.** Hurv should inspire immediate discomfort with his flat measuring gaze, unease at the Cassalanter devotional symbol beneath his groundskeeper's clothes, and alarm when the party learns what "becomes shadows at night" actually means.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/gralhunds/04-hurv-taldred.md:16: **Motivations.** Hurv wants to protect Gralhund Villa from intrusion, fulfill his obligations to the Cassalanter cult that gave his life meaning, and eliminate anything that crosses the yard perimeter without authorization.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/gralhunds/04-hurv-taldred.md:22: **Persona.** To the world, Hurv Taldred is the Gralhund Villa groundskeeper — taciturn, competent, and territorial about the yard. To those who probe his loyalties, the Cassalanter devotional mark beneath his shirt and his behavior on nights when the villa's guests worship suggest he answers to more than the Gralhunds. Deep down, he does not regard the cultist identity and the groundskeeper identity as separate things; both exist to keep the grounds clean.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/gralhunds/04-hurv-taldred.md:26: **Relationships.** Hurv is employed by the Gralhunds as yard security and serves the Cassalanter cult with equal devotion, the principal threat in G2 during **Gralhund Villa** infiltrations, and more dangerous after sunset than most parties will expect on first contact.

campaign/setting/notable-figures/harpers/08-maxeene.md:26: **Relationships.** Maxeene is a Harper intelligence asset who understands and speaks Common thanks to a permanent enchantment from a Harper-sympathetic druid, the subject of Harper mission one (**The Talking Mare**, which usually ends with her moved to Hessa Dorn's stable on Brondar's Way), and the best passive surveillance available to anyone who remembers to ask her what she heard at the hitching post outside the Cassalanter Villa last tenday.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/independents-adversaries/01-dagult-neverember.md:7: > - **Featured in:** **Finding Floon**, **Trollskull Alley**, **Fireball!**, **Gralhund Villa**, **Faction Outposts**, **Cassalanter Villa**, **Vault of Dragons**, **The Stone's Other Master**, **Gralhund Villa (location)**, **Zhentarim Warehouse**, **Cassalanter Villa (location)**
campaign/setting/notable-figures/independents-adversaries/02-the-black-viper.md:8: > - **Featured in:** **Faction Outposts**, **Cassalanter Villa**, **Sea Maidens Faire**, **Vault of Dragons**, **The Black Viper Investigation**, **The Poisoned Delivery**, **Silencing Skeemo**, **Cassalanter Villa (location)**
campaign/setting/notable-figures/independents-adversaries/02-the-black-viper.md:26: **Relationships.** The Black Viper is Lady Esvele Rosznar of House Rosznar (a member the house would prefer to forget if she were caught), an independent cat burglar with no faction affiliation, a wildcard who can be recruited for a Cassalanter Villa infiltration with a DC 14 Persuasion or Intimidation check, and a recurring complication in any noble estate the party decides to rob. Her base of operations — the Viper Den — sits directly above the Brandath Crypts on the approaches to the Vault of Dragons, which means she has mapped the crypt access in more detail than any living person outside the Cassalanter household. In **Cassalanter Villa** she attends the Cassalanter event as a social guest and slips away mid-dinner to rob her hosts, creating a Batman/Catwoman dynamic with any party also running reconnaissance that evening. In **Vault of Dragons** her knowledge of the crypt is an asset worth cultivating or a complication worth managing. She has been mapping the Brandath Crypts for three months and has located the vault entrance, but cannot open it — she has no Stone, no Eyes, no dragon to negotiate with. Her plan is to be present when someone else opens it and take the most portable, highest-value items before the gold conversation bogs down. She is not interested in the gold itself: 500,000 gp weighs ten thousand pounds and cannot be moved by a lone cat burglar. She is interested in whatever magical artifacts, gems, and documentation are inside. When the party arrives at the crypts in **Vault of Dragons**, Esvele is already there doing reconnaissance. She does not antagonize them unless they attempt to cut her out of the take entirely.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/independents-allies/02-renaer-neverember.md:8: > - **Featured in:** **Finding Floon**, **Trollskull Alley**, **Fireball!**, **Gralhund Villa**, **Cassalanter Villa**, **Vault of Dragons**, **The Stone's Other Master**, **The Dragon in the Harbor**, **Gralhund Villa (location)**, **Zhentarim Warehouse**, **Xanathar Sewer Hideout**
campaign/setting/notable-figures/independents-allies/19-aurinax.md:8: > - **Featured in:** **Fireball!**, **Xanathar's Lair**, **Cassalanter Villa**, **Kolat Towers**, **Vault of Dragons**
campaign/setting/notable-figures/independents-allies/20-kalain.md:26: **Relationships.** Kalain was a former intimate of Dagult Neverember whose abandonment ended both her career and her peace of mind; her tower contains correspondence bearing directly on the Vault of Dragons; her locked document case holds intelligence — the first concrete Cassalanter-to-vault link, a vault-opening clue, and an unsigned letter confirming Neverember's deliberate separation of Stone and Eyes — that is among the most consequential in the campaign, available to any party with enough patience to earn it.

campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/03-vevette-blackwater.md:22: **Persona.** To the world, Vevette is a swashbuckler with fiery red hair streaked platinum that can glow in the dark on her command. Charming in public, cruel in private. To those she trusts, she carries Seffia Naelryke's visiting card, having rebuffed a Cassalanter backchannel offer she suspected was a trap. Deep down, she is counting on the law to protect her from serious harm if captured, and she knows she has done nothing to warrant arrest.

campaign/setting/organizations/10-cassalanters.md:20: - **A Good Name Is Worth Keeping:** The charity is real. The family funds temples, orphanages and civic improvements, and Victoro is known as a worshipper of Lathander. Their warmth, including their warmth toward tieflings, is specific and genuine. Victoro keeps his philanthropist reputation as a working part of the plan, and the Harpers, who watch the family's charitable work, have found nothing wrong with it for years.
campaign/setting/organizations/10-cassalanters.md:21: - **The Family Comes First:** The love for the twins is real and drives every decision. Victoro and Ammalia would do anything to save Terenzio and Elzerina, and in a fight Ammalia grabs the twins and runs.
campaign/setting/organizations/10-cassalanters.md:23: - **A Contract Is a Contract:** Asmodeus does not negotiate with mortals who have already signed, so the family works inside the terms. They hunt for loopholes, never for release. Victoro is the family's priest and Ammalia makes the final calls.
campaign/setting/organizations/10-cassalanters.md:26: - **Service Is Worship:** The cult's members fold devotion into the work they do for the family. Tissina Khyret attends Ammalia, Nana Rosse minds the twins, Jandar Chergoba cooks and Seffia Naelryke runs field operations, and none of them sees a conflict between the job and the god.
campaign/setting/organizations/10-cassalanters.md:31: Victoro and Ammalia Cassalanter are Waterdeep aristocrats who made a devil's bargain. The details vary depending on who is speaking. Three years ago, they forged a pact with Asmodeus, trading their children's souls for power and prosperity. Their twins, Terenzio and Elzerina, are eight years old. On their ninth birthday, Flamerule 11, the pact comes due. Founders' Day falls ten days before, on Flamerule 1; that is when the Cassalanters plan to collect the ninety-nine souls.

campaign/setting/organizations/10-cassalanters.md:58: The Cassalanters are a noble family of Waterdeep and among the city's most prominent philanthropists. Victoro, a half-elf banker and worshipper of Lathander, and Ammalia, a wizard known for driving a hard bargain and for keeping the most beautiful butterfly garden in Waterdeep, fund temples, orphanages and civic improvements across the city. The family lives in a villa in the Sea Ward, is publicly known for its ancestral devotion to Siamorphe, and hosts public galas during Waukeentide, and the younger twins, Terenzio and Elzerina, are often seen at play in the gardens. Their eldest son is said to be at school in a faraway city, and more than one Waterdhavian has wondered aloud how a family can be so gracious, so prosperous and so fortunate all at once.
campaign/setting/organizations/10-cassalanters.md:63: > - **Origins:** The Cassalanter name has stood in Waterdeep's noble registry for several generations. The Lords of Waterdeep ennobled the house in 1248 DR, about 244 years before the campaign, when they granted titles to several merchant houses (Forgotten Realms Wiki, *City of Splendors* 2e). The wiki lists Waukeen, Siamorphe and Asmodeus as the house's favored deities. Financial ruin was the trigger for the bargain, which was made three years ago, about 1489 DR. The deal also bought power, good health and long life.
campaign/setting/organizations/10-cassalanters.md:66: > - **Chain of command:** Ammalia makes the final calls and Victoro has stopped questioning this. Willifort Crowelle reports to Ammalia on everything that happens in the villa. Seffia Naelryke's response team answers to Lord Cassalanter, and Rella Asp and Ivrala Sorn report to Willifort.
campaign/setting/organizations/10-cassalanters.md:68: >   - The Order of the Gauntlet is suspicious of the family and will move against the family once it has solid evidence. Sister Valdra holds three independent pieces of Cassalanter intelligence.

campaign/setting/organizations/10-cassalanters.md:72: > - **Caladorn Cassalanter:** Victoro's father. The wiki has him a Masked Lord and later Open Lord of Waterdeep, by 1399 DR (*Blackstaff Tower*, 2e and 3e). The family uses the portrait of the former Masked Lord and hero of the North as a symbol of civic virtue. His ghost waits below the villa and is the reason the way to break the contract lies beneath it.
campaign/setting/organizations/10-cassalanters.md:78: > - **The pact:** Victoro and Ammalia traded the souls of their children to Asmodeus for wealth compressed from three generations into one and for political access that normally takes a family centuries to build. They discussed it, understood what they were signing, and signed, three years ago. The pact covers three children: Osvaldo, already taken, and the twins.
campaign/setting/organizations/10-cassalanters.md:79: > - **Osvaldo:** The eldest son's soul was claimed first, when he was nine, the age at which the contract claims each child, and he is now twelve and a chain devil held in the soundproofed attic of the villa, room C24, never the basement or the temple. Ammalia visits weekly, sitting in the room outside the pentagram to read to him while he curses her and weeps. Victoro has not visited in months. Willifort knows. Two imps are always present. Terenzio and Elzerina have been told Osvaldo is at school. Had the contract run its course, the twins were to become lemures (Forgotten Realms Wiki, Victoro Cassalanter, from *Dragon Heist*, 5e).
campaign/setting/organizations/10-cassalanters.md:80: > - **The feast:** On Founders' Day, Flamerule 1, the family plans to poison 99 guests at a feast to collect the ninety-nine souls. Ammalia is the architect of the feast and Jandar Chergoba, the villa's head chef, is the person who sees that the poison is distributed correctly.
campaign/setting/organizations/10-cassalanters.md:81: > - **The deadline:** The pact comes due on Flamerule 11, the twins' ninth birthday. Founders' Day on Flamerule 1 is the feast and Ammalia's real deadline. It is her operational window, chosen for the civic cover and for the time left to verify the terms, and it does not appear in the contract.

campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:216: ***Cassalanter Name-Drop Through Renaer.*** Renaer's aside about the Cassalanters' property acquisitions near the Brandath tombs is designed to sit unexamined in player notes until Arc G. He does not frame it as a threat or a suspicion — he raises it as an oddity he cannot explain. Players who track it will revisit the detail when the Cassalanter Villa becomes a heist target. Players who do not track it will have it recapitulated by Renaer in Arc G when the connection becomes relevant. The name-drop is passive: it seeds without pressing. A first-level party has no use for it yet, and none is offered.
campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:237: 6. **Cassalanter name-drop delivery.** Renaer dialogue in Scene 3. He mentions the Cassalanters' property acquisitions near the Brandath tombs as an unexplained oddity, not a suspicion.

campaign/structure/arc-b-trollskull-alley.md:27: Between the renovation costs, the guild fees, and the neighbor who keeps filing complaints about rats, the city shows the party exactly what it costs to put down roots. Before the tavern opens, an invitation from the Sea Maidens Faire draws them to the Field of Triumph, where a carnival master named Zardoz Zord watches from a private box and Meloon Wardragon leads the opposing team in the final round. The arc's emotional high point is the Grand Opening — a full house, a poltergeist who has extended professional courtesy to the new owners, and an Ammalia Cassalanter attending as a generous patron, curious and charming and already counting the days to Founders' Day. The Twin Parades follow not long after, where something mechanical walks in one of the processions without explanation.
campaign/structure/arc-b-trollskull-alley.md:212: *The Cassalanter Bank Hook (New Content).* When the party's renovation financing requires a formal loan or credit arrangement, their paperwork passes through the Cassalanter Counting House — a Sea Ward banking operation with accounts throughout the North Ward. A warm and precise counting house clerk mentions that Lord and Lady Cassalanter take a personal interest in new businesses establishing themselves in the North Ward. Two days before the Grand Opening, a messenger delivers a cask of Athkatlan vintage wine with a card: *"From the house of Cassalanter. We look forward to patronizing your establishment."* No counting house clerk sends wine of this quality without instructions from above. **Thread: The Cassalanters Exist** (second path; the first path was Renaer's aside in Scene 3: "the Cassalanters are generous to causes they want to be seen supporting — keep that in mind").
campaign/structure/arc-b-trollskull-alley.md:220: *Ammalia Cassalanter (New Content).* Ammalia Cassalanter arrives without Lord Cassalanter. She is everything the wine gift promised: warm, generous, interested in the party as people. She stays two hours, learns everyone's name, and leaves with the social grace that makes a room feel larger after she's gone. She is a diabolist counting the days to Founders' Day. None of that is visible here. Her warmth is genuine — she finds capable people interesting, and she is very interested in people who have proven they can navigate a city that is doing its best to kill them. **Thread: The Cassalanters Exist** (third path). Cross-ref **Appendix A** (Ammalia Cassalanter profile).

campaign/structure/arc-b-trollskull-alley.md:286: 3. **Ammalia at the Grand Opening (Scene 6).** First appearance at the Grand Opening, preceded by the Cassalanter Bank Hook (credit note and wine gift two days prior). Her warmth is genuine and unambiguous; the diabolist framing is entirely DM-side.

campaign/structure/arc-b-trollskull-alley.md:315: - **Appendix A: NPC Roster** — All Trollskull Alley neighbor profiles (Section 1); Ryvarra (BD Section); Emmek Frewn and Shard Shunners; Ammalia Cassalanter; Willifort Crowelle; Filthy Meg (Section 3)
campaign/structure/arc-b-trollskull-alley.md:347: ## Ammalia Cassalanter's Introduction
campaign/structure/arc-b-trollskull-alley.md:349: ***Her Warmth Must Be Genuine.*** The temptation is to signal the Cassalanters as villains through dramatic irony — give Ammalia slightly too-bright smiles, odd conversational choices, a moment of coldness beneath the grace. Resist this entirely. Ammalia Cassalanter is a warm, generous, politically sophisticated woman who is also a cult leader preparing to sacrifice children to Asmodeus. These are not contradictory. The warmth is how she has always functioned in the world; the diabolism is a choice she made under duress and has not stepped back from. If players suspect her in Scene 5, it should be because they are suspicious people, not because the text gave them a signal. The dramatic irony of Arc G — watching a woman they genuinely liked negotiate a devil's contract — requires that the liking be earned honestly in Arc B.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:106: *Faction Response Teams Deploy.* All four villain factions move on the crime scene within hours. Xanathar Guild gazers post to watch the alley (DC 16 Perception to spot them); Manshoon's Zhent operatives run a parallel investigation through their own network; Bregan D'Aerthe observers activate conditionally, their level of engagement scaling with whether the party later reaches the Sea Maidens Faire; the Cassalanters send flowers and a warm handwritten condolence note — Ammalia's handwriting. Cross-ref Appendix E (faction response team rosters and behavior by arc).
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:191: | NW-03 | CR-03 | Marpenoth 12 | V. Cassalanter | Cassalanter Villa, Sea Ward | *VOID* |
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:196: Critical entry: NW-02, O. Gralhund, Saerdoun Street, North Ward. Notable entry: NW-03, V. Cassalanter, Cassalanter Villa, Sea Ward.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:200: *The Voided Cassalanter Entry. (New Content)* The complete ledger records a Cassalanter commission — placed, programmed, voided before delivery. Notation: "Buyer withdrew — unspecified concerns re: detection." Victoro Cassalanter suspected the nimblewrights might contain surveillance devices. He was correct. The clairvoyance crystal reference numbers in the ledger are the key: each purchased nimblewright has a corresponding crystal recording what it witnesses. Victoro pulled his order when he worked this out. This pays off in Arc G when the party discovers what the Gralhund unit's crystal captured.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:206: ### Scene 5: The Cassalanter Dinner (New Content)
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:208: **Purpose:** The Cassalanters make their first move. The party has met Ammalia before — she visited Trollskull Manor in Arc B and is a known patron. This dinner is something different: Victoro is present, the invitation arrived the day after the fireball, and the conversation has an agenda. Ammalia's warmth is unchanged and genuine. Victoro's interest in the Stone is new, careful, and undisguised only to those paying close attention. The party's response — how much they share, whether they accept — becomes the Cassalanters' operational picture for Arc G.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:212: *The Invitation.* Ammalia sends a personal note within a day of the fireball, handwritten on cream vellum in a clear, unhurried hand. She references the neighborhood tragedy, expresses concern for "those so near the incident on Ches 22nd," and invites the party to dinner at Cassalanter Villa the following evening. The letter mentions that Victoro is eager to meet them properly, which is the only signal that business will be discussed. There is no pressure: the invitation includes a footman's address for a reply.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:214: *The Villa.* Cassalanter Villa sits in the Sea Ward, set back from the street behind an iron gate and a garden whose topiary has been maintained for two generations. The house staff is attentive, formal, and impeccably briefed. The dining room seats twelve; tonight it is set for six. The children are "not well enough for company" — a detail offered once and not returned to. The food is excellent. The wine is exceptional. Everything is exactly right, and a party paying attention will find the precision slightly exhausting.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:216: *Ammalia at the Table.* She remembers every detail the party mentioned in Arc B — neighborhood politics, the state of Trollskull Manor, small personal observations — and asks after them with what reads as genuine interest. She is not performing warmth; she possesses it in excess and deploys it without calculation, which makes her harder to read than Victoro. A DC 14 Insight check reveals that she is watching to see how the party handles kindness, not suspicion: she is forming an opinion. The result of that opinion is Victoro's problem.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:218: *Victoro's Pitch.* After the first course, Victoro raises the fireball. He expresses condolences. He mentions that the family had some acquaintance with Lord Neverember's later projects and that the object Dalakhar was carrying has "historical and financial significance beyond what most people appreciate." He does not name the Stone of Golorr. He offers to share what the Cassalanters have learned — contacts, research, documents — in exchange for a first conversation if the party recovers the object. His language is courteous, precise, and carries no threat. He does not ask for the Stone. He asks to be in the room.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:220: *What the Cassalanters Know.* Victoro knows: Dalakhar worked for Neverember, the Stone is the key to a concealed vault, all three Eyes were separated from the Stone years ago, and the vault's physical entrance is in the Brandath family crypt. He does not share any of this at the dinner. His goal is assessment — he wants to know how much the party has found and whether they are worth cultivating.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:222: *What the Cassalanters Do Not Know.* Nobody at this table knows the Cassalanters are infernalists — not the Harpers, not Force Grey, not the party. Ammalia and Victoro do not volunteer anything about the children, the pact, or the shrine. The debt is the conversation they are not having. A DC 16 Insight check on Victoro reads him as a man who made a decision he cannot unmake and has arranged his life carefully around it. This is accurate. It yields nothing.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:225: - **Sharing intelligence:** The Cassalanters learn where the investigation points. Victoro updates his operational picture. Entering Arc G, he knows what the party found at Gralhund Villa and what they did there. Cassalanter response teams are positioned accordingly.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:226: - **Deflecting:** Victoro notes the restraint and interprets it as operational caution — a quality he respects. He does not press. Entering Arc G, he treats the party as capable but unaligned.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:227: - **Accepting the arrangement:** Victoro follows up within two days with a sealed letter summarizing what the Cassalanters know — no vault specifics; he is not foolish. Entering Arc G, the party has a prior relationship and a document trail. The alliance track opens.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:228: - **Refusing outright or treating the dinner as hostile:** Ammalia does not change expression. Victoro sees them out personally. Entering Arc G, the party is a complication rather than an asset.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:230: **Tone:** Layered hospitality. Every comfort is real and every comfort is a negotiating position. Ammalia is the warmth; Victoro is the patience. The party is not in danger. They are being evaluated by people who are very good at it.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:236: **Purpose:** Optional investigation branch rewarding thorough parties with Grand Game history, a Cassalanter connection through Kalain, and vault-opening clues that pay off in Arc J. Non-essential for reaching Gralhund Villa — gated behind proactive behavior, not hidden by design.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:271: - **Lord Victoro Cassalanter visited a few weeks ago, asking many of the same questions Dalakhar had asked years before — the vault, Neverember's last projects, whether she kept his papers.** She will not volunteer this. Asked directly whether anyone else has come asking about Neverember, she confirms it. He was polite. She found his politeness the most unsettling thing about the visit. This is the first concrete Cassalanter-to-vault link.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:305: - Victoro Cassalanter has been actively investigating the vault and offered alliance through Ammalia's dinner invitation.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:334: 15. **Cassalanter dinner before Gralhund Villa.** First direct villain contact. Ammalia's warmth is established behavior from Arc B. No mention of children, the infernal pact, or Asmodeus.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:355: - **Arc G: Cassalanter Villa** — Cassalanter dinner in Scene 5 is the first direct villain contact. The voided nimblewright ledger entry establishes that Victoro correctly suspected surveillance devices. Kalain's account of Victoro's visit (Scene 5, optional) is the first concrete vault-to-Cassalanter link.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:358: - **Appendix A: NPC Roster** — Profiles required for: Barnibus Blastwind, Saeth Cromley, Fala Lefaliir, Jezrynne Hornraven, Renaer Neverember, Valetta, Nim, Captain Zord (Jarlaxle), Myl Dunpier, Kalain, Urstul Floxin, Ammalia and Victoro Cassalanter, Davil Starsong, Tashlyn Yafeera.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:386: ***The First Arc Where All Four Villain Factions Pursue the Same Object.*** Arc A establishes the Xanathar Guild and Manshoon's Zhentarim as adversaries. Arc B introduces the Cassalanters as patrons and Bregan D'Aerthe as observers. Arc C is the first arc where all four factions reach for the same object at the same time in the same city. The design makes this concrete rather than atmospheric: Xanathar gazers in the alley, Zhent parallel investigation, BD conditional observers scaling with party choices at the Faire, Cassalanter condolence note and dinner invitation. These are not flavor. They are the Grand Game becoming visible for the first time — the moment the party understands they are not the only investigators in Waterdeep, and that the city is full of people who already know more than they do.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:392: ***The Dock Ward as Deliberate Redirect.*** The Faire visit feels like a stall because it is one — the Gralhund nimblewright is not there. This is intentional. The frustration of following a real lead to a productive-but-incomplete result is exactly the kind of experience good investigative fiction creates. The payoff arrives the moment the party moves north: the detector's behavior changes on Saerdoun Street in a way that makes the Faire visit retroactively coherent. The intelligence gathered there — the ledger, the Cassalanter entry, the BD private meeting, the Jarlaxle identification — makes the detour worthwhile in ways the party cannot fully assess until later.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:394: ## The Cassalanter Approach
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:396: ***The Cassalanters Make Their Move.*** Arc B establishes the Cassalanters as warm, generous patrons — Ammalia a known presence at Trollskull Manor. Arc C delivers the first approach with an agenda: the dinner invitation arrives the morning after the fireball, and Victoro is at the table. Ammalia's warmth is unchanged; her role is to make the party comfortable enough that Victoro's question feels collaborative rather than predatory. The dinner is a genuine attempt to recruit capable people before someone else does — and an intelligence-gathering operation. The party's response shapes what the Cassalanters know when Arc G begins. This scene solves a pacing problem in the original campaign where the Cassalanters appear in Arc B as patrons and vanish entirely until the Arc G heist, with no intervening contact to bridge that gap. The Arc C dinner makes the connection without revealing anything premature — no pact, no children, no Asmodeus.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:400: ***Non-Essential, Consequential.*** Scene 5 cannot block the party's path to Gralhund Villa — parties that skip it still reach Arc D. But parties that pursue it gain Kalain's account, the first concrete Cassalanter-to-vault link, the vault-opening clue, and the unsigned Eyes letter confirming Neverember's deliberate separation of Stone and Eyes. These are among the most consequential intelligence finds in the entire campaign, gated behind exactly the kind of proactive habits the investigation arcs are trying to build: exhaustive canvassing, leaving contact information, following up on letters addressed to dead men. The design rewards the investigative instinct without penalizing its absence.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:164: **G16 (Master Bedroom).** Yalah's private chambers. The door can be barred from inside (Hrabbaz does this automatically when the alarm sounds). Locked trunk at the foot of the bed: DC 15 thieves' tools. Secret compartment inside the trunk: DC 15 Perception to find. Inside: two holy symbols of Asmodeus (silver, engraved) and two liturgical robes in red and gold with Cassalanter house embroidery. This is the first concrete evidence that the Gralhunds and Cassalanters share a cult — not a business relationship or a political alliance, but actual Asmodean religious affiliation. It pays off in Arc G.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:241: *The Wooden Trunk.* At the foot of the bed. Locked (DC 15 thieves' tools). Appears to contain clothes and shoes. DC 15 Perception to spot the false bottom. Inside: two holy symbols of Asmodeus in chased silver, two liturgical robes in red and gold with Cassalanter house embroidery worked into the collar. This is the first physical evidence connecting the Gralhunds and the Cassalanters as members of the same Asmodean cult — not political allies, not business partners, but worshippers. It pays off in Arc G.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:353: - **The Gralhund–Cassalanter Cult Link:** In hand if G16's compartment was found. Two holy symbols of Asmodeus, two liturgical robes with Cassalanter embroidery. Pays off in Arc G.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:419: - **Arc G: Cassalanter Villa** — The Asmodeus compartment in G16 (Scene 3) is the first concrete Gralhund–Cassalanter cult link. Seeds the party's understanding of Victoro and Ammalia's religious affiliation before they enter the villa.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:15: Ten outposts are distributed across Waterdeep's wards, maintained by four factions, each one a data point in the web of the Grand Game. The party cannot storm the lairs blind — Xanathar's sanctum, Cassalanter Villa's temple, the Sea Maidens Faire, and Kolat Towers are not targets until the party knows enough to attempt them. The outposts are the research: entering a Xanatharian front to find how the Guild accesses its own lair, or breaking into a cultist's apartment to find a map the Cassalanters spent months assembling.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:35: That calculation is no longer accurate. Neverember is in Neverwinter. The parties holding his Eyes have been in Waterdeep long enough to develop their own plans for whatever the Stone might reveal. Jarlaxle Baenre holds his Eye aboard the *Scarlet Marpenoth*, where it serves as leverage and curiosity in equal measure. The Cassalanter family, working through a network of cult agents and infernal diviners, located a second Eye buried in the crypt of a noblewoman who died without heirs, and extracted it. Xanathar holds the last in Sylgar's fishbowl in the lair beneath Skullport. Zhentarim envoys stole it from the Protector's Enclave in Neverwinter and carried it to Xanathar as a gift. Xanathar killed them, kept the stone, and has never learned what it is.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:63: *Stone Knowledge Expansion (Forward Note — Implement in Heist Arcs).* The Alexandrian Remix establishes that the Stone's knowledge expands incrementally as each Eye is restored. With zero Eyes, it yields only the fragmentary impressions above. With one Eye, it reveals additional details. With two, more still. All three Eyes unlock the vault's precise location and command phrase. This expansion belongs in the lair heists (**Xanathar's Lair**, **Cassalanter Villa**, **Sea Maidens Faire**) where Eyes are actually recovered — not in this quest. This note exists so the mechanic is not overlooked when those arcs are drafted. Cross-ref `sources/8. The Eyes of the Stone.pdf` for the authoritative expansion table.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:81: *Mirt (Harpers — renown 1+).* The Harpers have been watching the Cassalanter family for years, and Mirt's summary reflects sustained, inconclusive surveillance. The Cassalanters give more to charity than houses twice their wealth, never miss a temple service, and have not made a single political enemy in a decade. "Nobody is that clean," Mirt says, without pleasure. He does not know what they are hiding. The Harpers have found no evidence of wrongdoing — only the complete absence of any wrongdoing at all, which in Waterdeep's political ecology is itself suspicious. He recommends starting with Xanathar outposts on the grounds that the Guild's paranoia makes early strikes more effective than late ones. His unstated reason: the Harpers have already exhausted their Xanathar intelligence, and the Cassalanter question is the one he cannot answer and cannot let go.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:85: *Melannor Fellbranch (Emerald Enclave — renown 1+).* Jeryth Phaulkon has been monitoring a disturbance in the Southern Ward for two weeks. The resonance is infernal in nature — a persistent presence that touches the ley lines near Coachlamp Lane in a way Jeryth describes as a wound that isn't healing. The Enclave knows the disturbance is infernal but has no lead on the source. "Jeryth felt it from Phaulkonmere. It is not natural and it is not stopping." Melannor is framing this as an environmental problem, not an investigation lead. Parties who trace the resonance to its source will find the Cassalanter chain's entry point. Melannor does not know this.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:119: **Cassalanter outposts:** The infernal presence near Coachlamp Lane is detectable by any character with Divine Sense within 60 feet of the Converted Windmill, and confirmable with *detect evil and good*. Parties without such abilities can find the location through Coachlamp Lane neighborhood inquiry — neighbors report strange smells, unusual visitors at odd hours, a couple who never quite fit in (DC 13 Charisma, no faction required).
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:127: *Cross-Faction Spillover.* Xanathar and Manshoon are actively at war; each monitors the other's losses. Hitting a Xanathar outpost raises Manshoon's alert tier by 1, and vice versa — both factions assume the other is responsible before they conclude it was the party. Cassalanter imp surveillance of the Sea Maidens Faire creates a reciprocal intelligence channel; Cassalanter losses alert Jarlaxle that something is moving against infernal-adjacent operations in the city. See **Running the Villains** (GM Guide) for full inter-faction dynamics.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:129: *Founders' Day Clock.* Every outpost entry includes a time estimate: how many in-game days the operation consumes. Encounter-plus operations run 1–3 days. Mini-heist operations run 3–7 days. Total duration: two to four tendays. The party learns about Founders' Day (Flamerule 1st) through Cassalanter intelligence and faction contacts, not through this section. The GM tracks the calendar and makes the approaching deadline legible through environmental detail — festival preparations appearing in the wards, Cassalanter documents referencing a family obligation in Flamerule, contacts mentioning the Open Lord's Founders' Day calendar.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:147: *Step 1 — Gather Information.* Three paths to Terasse Estate before the party enters it: the *Instructions for Gladiatorial Weaponry* found at Grinda Garloth's (names Terasse as a delivery point for exotic arms); the mechanical beholder letter at Orb Confectioners (names Terasse as the delivery address and dates it); or, if **Cassalanter Villa** has been completed first, a Cassalanter Villa report referencing the gladiatorial supply chain. Parties lacking these documents can attempt entry by bluffing past the door with the healing passphrase (DC 18 Deception if improvising) or by obtaining the passphrase from an interrogated Xanathar response team member.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:153: *Step 4 — The Operation.* Primary objectives: recover the *Instructions for Gladiatorial Weaponry* from Keln's desk (Area 6–7), which names Grinda Garloth as the supplier and Ahmaergo as the authorizing agent; recover the *Injury Reports* logbook (Area 5 Upper Landing), which contains an entry noting a wound caused by "incautious observation of the windmill on Coachlamp Lane" — a cross-faction clue pointing toward the Cassalanter outpost chain; recover the *Ill Man's Note* (Area 8–10 Sick Rooms), which contains the Orb Confectioners passphrase in verse. Optional: follow a guest being transported to the gladiatorial event. The trap door in the kitchen leads to an underground dock; guests are blindfolded before descent and transported by boat to Area X4 of Xanathar's Lair. This route is a genuine lair entry method, but arriving via gladiatorial transport means arriving unprepared, blindfolded, and unarmed. See the **Xanathar's Guild** Factions guide page for keyed area details.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:237: ### Scene 6: Cassalanter Outposts
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:249: *Approach.* The shrine's location is actively concealed; it requires at least one prior lead to find. Four paths run through documents or contacts: the Converted Windmill's meeting note ("Meeting Ches 17th, the shrine on Aveen Street") — circular only if the party finds the windmill first; the *Instructions from Lord Cassalanter* found inside the shrine on the apprentice wizard — circular if already inside; a BD report from the Sea Maidens Faire referencing imp activity in the North Ward, if **Sea Maidens Faire** has been completed first; or proactive Harper assistance — Mirt can connect the party with an Enclave contact who has been narrowing the Southern Ward infernal resonance to a North Ward secondary signature. Order of the Gauntlet Missions 4 and 5 give a fifth, independent path that needs no outpost document. The chandler's cellar on the Field Ward side of Aveen Street is a back way into the shrine, and its cellar door in the yard answers three knocks.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:255: Area 2 — Styxian Shrine: anyone who lingers without succeeding on a DC 14 Wisdom saving throw loses eight hours of recent memory. The loss feels like waking from a dream — not a gap, but a seamless continuity that has been quietly rewritten. An apprentice wizard prays here; he carries *Instructions from Lord Cassalanter* directing imp surveillance of the Sea Maidens Faire.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:257: Area 4 — Chapter House: an animate object statue guards the room (50 hp, AC 10, +6 to hit, 2d20+2 damage). On a shelf: *Arn's Book of Worship*, naming Arn Xalrondar and citing his address as "the Windmill upon Coachlamp Lane." This is the bridge between the two Cassalanter outposts.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:259: Area 6 — Mirror of the Fiend: a tarnished floor mirror bearing an infernal sigil. DC 13 History identifies the sigil as a noble house marking; DC 16 identifies the house as Cassalanter. This is the arc's first solid evidence linking the Cassalanters to infernal operations — not a rumor, not suspicious charity, but a physical object bearing their family crest inside an Asmodean shrine.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:261: Area 7 — Nine Hells Mural: a detailed depiction of Avernus. A DC 15 Arcana check recognizes architectural elements consistent with Asmodean temple design — foreshadowing what the party will encounter beneath the villa in **Cassalanter Villa**.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:265: *Intel Yield.* Arn's Book of Worship names Arn and the Coachlamp Lane windmill — the bridge to the Converted Windmill. The Cassalanter mirror sigil provides the family identification. The *Instructions from Lord Cassalanter* confirms an active Cassalanter–BD surveillance operation. Cultists questioned under pressure name Cassalanter Villa and describe Victoro as the operation's director. A party that recovers all four pieces of intelligence now knows more about the Cassalanters than the Harpers have gathered in years.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:273: *Southern Ward, Coachlamp Lane.* — **Street-level entry point for the Cassalanter chain** (for parties with Divine Sense).
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:275: *Step 1 — Gather Information.* Five paths to the Converted Windmill: Seffia Naelryke's visiting card found on Vevette Blackwater from a Zhentarim response team (Seffia approached Vevette to open a communication channel; Vevette rebuffed her, suspecting a trap, but kept the card); Arn's Book of Worship at the Asmodean Shrine; the *Injury Reports* logbook at Terasse Estate ("the windmill on Coachlamp Lane"); a Cassalanter Villa report if **Cassalanter Villa** is completed first; or the infernal presence detected by Divine Sense within 60 feet of the building (DC 12 Wisdom for any character with Divine Sense; no faction required). Parties without Divine Sense can reach the windmill through Coachlamp Lane neighborhood inquiry — neighbors report strange smells, unusual visitors after midnight, a couple who never quite fit in (DC 13 Charisma; no faction required).
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:281: *Step 4 — The Operation.* Area W7 — Secure Apartment: beneath the bed, a note reading "Meeting Ches 17th, the shrine on Aveen Street." This links the two Cassalanter outposts for parties who haven't visited the shrine yet.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:283: Area W8 — Room of Rituals: a blood pentagram drawn in salt and ash. A DC 15 Arcana check reveals it as a demonic divination circle. The working notes describe a search for "Neverember," "Golorr," and "that which has been lost to the waters of Lethe." A desk beside the pentagram holds the most important document in this quest: **a map of Waterdeep with the Brandath Crypt circled in red ink.** This map is the primary clue path to **Vault of Dragons**'s physical entrance. Seffia carries *A Letter from Victoro* confirming the Third Eye was found "in the crypt of Lady Brandath" — this letter requires either stealth or a confrontation to obtain.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:285: *Step 5 — Getaway.* Seffia Naelryke (cult fanatic) and Arn Xalrondar (cult fanatic) with the sentry imp. Seffia fights with conviction — she is not a frightened acolyte but a dedicated operative who has worked years toward this goal. Arn follows her lead. The imp's ability to escape unseen is the primary complication; the party must neutralize it or accept the escalation consequence. **Escalation: Cassalanters move to Lockdown (Tier 4) if the shrine has already been hit; move to Alert (Tier 3) if this is the first Cassalanter outpost.** Response team deploys within 1d4 days. If the imp escapes, the Cassalanters know what was taken.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:351: *Response Team Compositions.* See the relevant Factions guide pages for full compositions and carried documents. Every team leader carries faction-specific documents that function as intelligence opportunities: Zhentarim teams carry directives naming Brindul Alley or Kolat Towers; Xanathar teams carry operational correspondence from Nar'l or Ahmaergo; BD teams carry playbills or coded messages in Drow; Cassalanter teams carry instructions from Lord Cassalanter. The party should treat every response team encounter as a potential document recovery, not just a combat encounter.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:353: *Cross-Faction Response Team Encounters.* Xanathar and Manshoon teams may encounter each other during this quest — both factions are moving through the same wards. Witnessing these confrontations without engaging either side generates intelligence about both factions simultaneously. BD teams prefer misdirection over lethal force; they are more likely to shadow the party than confront them directly. Cassalanter teams vary by the party's relationship with the family.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:355: *The Cassalanter Dual Mode.* The Cassalanters respond differently depending on whether the party has accepted Ammalia's **Fireball!** dinner invitation and maintained a working relationship. Working with the Cassalanters: Seffia's team assists or at minimum does not interfere. Opposing or refusing them: imps maintain constant surveillance, Seffia's team competes at **Xanathar's Lair** operations, and Willifort Crowelle makes social approaches that escalate to assassination attempts if the party actively works against the family. See the **Cassalanters** Factions guide page (Response Teams section).
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:369: *Mirt (Harpers).* Full intelligence briefing. If Xanathar outposts were hit, Mirt asks specifically about Nihiloor references — the Harper file on the mind flayer is extensive, and any new document strengthens the case for Watch action. If Cassalanter outposts were hit and the party brings the Mirror of the Fiend sigil identification, the Cassalanter cult documents, or the cultists' testimony, Mirt's response is: relief, then urgency. He has been building suspicion for years without evidence. What the party has found in one outpost run is more concrete than everything he gathered in three years of surveillance. "I knew it couldn't just be charity." He does not have enough to move on the Cassalanters legally — he needs **Cassalanter Villa** for that — but the calculus on Founders' Day has changed.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:395: Total **Faction Outposts**: 1 Milestone Point, for the first completed chain; a second chain earns intelligence, not points. The party remains 4th level through all of **Faction Outposts**, at 10 cumulative points. A party can attempt any lair heist (**Xanathar's Lair**, **Cassalanter Villa**, **Sea Maidens Faire**, or **Kolat Towers**) after completing just one chain, or even none; this quest's outposts are the recommended on-ramp, not a hard gate. See the Milestone section in **Xanathar's Lair** (or any lair heist's) for how the heist phase carries the party from 4th to 7th level.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:397: *Transition.* The party holds enough intelligence to attempt at least one lair heist. The GM reviews each faction's escalation tier and communicates it through environmental detail: Dock Ward patrols are heavier than last tenday; a Castle Ward merchant notes that the old tower on the hill has gone dark; the Sea Maidens Faire hasn't performed in a week. Party chooses which lair first. **Xanathar's Lair**, **Cassalanter Villa**, **Sea Maidens Faire**, and **Kolat Towers** are available in any order.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:399: *Founders' Day Clock.* This quest consumes two to four tendays. Founders' Day is 25–45 days away when the party enters the lair phase. The deadline enters the party's awareness through festival preparations appearing in the wards, Cassalanter documents referencing a family obligation in Flamerule, and contacts mentioning the Open Lord's Founders' Day calendar in passing.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:421: 9. **Chain completion defined.** Two outposts of the same faction plus one piece of actionable lair intelligence. The first completed chain awards 1 Milestone Point (cumulative 10), not enough to reach level 5. Level 5 is reached on the first completed lair heist (**Xanathar's Lair**, **Cassalanter Villa**, **Sea Maidens Faire**, or **Kolat Towers**).
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:448: - **Fireball!** — The Cassalanter Approach scene establishes the dinner invitation and the party's existing relationship with the family before this quest's outpost discoveries.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:450: - **Cassalanter Villa** — Cassalanter outpost chain (Scene 6) feeds into **Cassalanter Villa**. The Brandath Crypt map and Victoro's letter are cross-quest documents. Esvele Rosznar in **Cassalanter Villa** provides a backup vault-location path.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:471: ## The Cassalanter Discovery
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:473: ***The Factions Don't Know.*** The Cassalanters' infernal operations have been intentionally withheld from every faction contact — including Mirt, including the full Harper intelligence apparatus. Their cover is the best in Waterdeep: no enemies made, no traceable crimes committed, nothing but admiration from every quarter. Mirt's "nobody is that clean" is as close as external observation gets. The purpose of this choice: it makes the players the first people in Waterdeep to uncover the truth, through their own investigation, rather than receiving it as briefing material from a contact. The Mirror of the Fiend, the cultists' testimony, Victoro's signed letter at the Windmill — the party assembles the picture one piece at a time, the way investigators are supposed to. Discovery earned is worth more than discovery given.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:475: ***This Quest's Discovery vs. Cassalanter Villa's Revelation.*** This quest and **Cassalanter Villa** deliver different things. In this quest, thorough investigators learn that the Cassalanters maintain infernal cult operations, that Victoro directs them, and that there is a specific Eye hidden at Brandath Crypt. What they do not yet know: the pact's terms, the children's involvement, the Founders' Day deadline. **Cassalanter Villa** delivers the full horror. This quest is the evidence that makes **Cassalanter Villa** meaningful — "we suspected it, and it is worse than we suspected" lands harder than "we were briefed and it is bad."
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:491: ***Lair Entrance Verification.*** Four lair entrances, all reachable from this quest's intelligence: Xanathar's Lair — Nar'l's guildsign trail, gladiatorial transport via Terasse, Ott Steeltoes as guide (three paths); Kolat Towers — Yellowspire teleportation circle, captured pass-amulet from Avareen, Agorn's amulet traded under duress (three paths); Cassalanter Villa — Ammalia's **Fireball!** dinner invitation, Cassalanter cultist testimony from this quest's outposts, Esvele Rosznar in **Cassalanter Villa** (three paths, the last requiring no completion of this quest at all); Sea Maidens Faire — Fenerus's decoded messages, Malcolm Brizzenbright's testimony, Xanathar's Lair report from **Xanathar's Lair** (three paths).
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:254: **If this is the first Eye restored (neither **Cassalanter Villa** nor **Sea Maidens Faire** completed).** This is the scene's centerpiece. The GM should pause the action here. The party has been carrying an object. They are about to discover they've been carrying a consciousness.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:260: **Sharpened Impressions.** The fragmentary images from **Faction Outposts**, Scene 1 clarify. The "red light through colored glass, incense smoke, children's laughter" resolves: the Stone shows a villa in the Sea Ward — a large private estate with a temple beneath it, stone floor engraved with a sigil the party may recognize as the Cassalanter house mark. The children's laughter has a hollow quality now. The "salt air and creaking wood, dark-skinned elf" resolves: a ship in Waterdeep harbor, flagged with a theater company's banner. A bald elf stands at the prow, watching the city with the patience of someone who has been waiting a long time and expects to wait longer. Parties who hit the Sea Maidens Faire or Cassalanter outposts in **Faction Outposts** will connect these images immediately. Parties who didn't have enough to investigate.
