campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:271: - The remaining Eye's location resolves fully. Which impression sharpens depends on which arc the party completed first. If **Cassalanter Villa** was first (already raided), the final impression is: "A ship in the harbor. The Scarlet Marpenoth. A bald elf with a year of patience." If **Sea Maidens Faire** was first (Jarlaxle's Eye already recovered), the final impression is: "A villa in the Sea Ward. A temple beneath it, stone floor engraved with the Cassalanter house mark. The children's laughter has a hollow quality." The Stone identifies each Eye by proximity-sense, not by name.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:276: **If this is the third Eye restored (both **Cassalanter Villa** and **Sea Maidens Faire** already completed).** The Stone goes still, then speaks with sudden, precise clarity. The fragmentary impressions are gone. The vault's location, the command phrase, and the nature of its guardian resolve in full. Run the 3-Eye disclosure scene from **Vault of Dragons** at this point. Quest order does not change the awakening, only its timing.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:288: **Cassalanters.** Imp surveillance detects unusual activity around Skullport entry points. If the relationship with the party is adversarial, Victoro's network flags the event. The Cassalanters don't know what was retrieved, but they know something changed.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:302: *Founders' Day Clock.* This quest consumes 1–2 tendays total: several days of intelligence briefing and casing (Scenes 1–3), 1–3 days for the operation and escape (Scenes 4–5), and 2–4 days of recovery and debrief (Scene 6). Update the campaign calendar. The GM communicates the advancing deadline through specific environmental details: festival preparations in the Market Ward are accelerating; a Cassalanter document recovered from the lair carries a date; a faction contact mentions the Open Lord's Founders' Day schedule is filling.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:304: *Transition.* The party holds the Stone of Golorr and Eye #1. The Stone can talk. It wants to be whole. Two Eyes remain; the sharpened impressions point toward a Sea Ward villa and a ship in the harbor. **Cassalanter Villa**, **Sea Maidens Faire**, and **Kolat Towers** are available in any order. The party's next choice is informed by the Stone's impressions, the faction debrief intelligence, and whatever documents they recovered from the lair.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:318: **Xanathar's Lair**, **Cassalanter Villa**, **Sea Maidens Faire**, and **Kolat Towers** each award the same 4 Milestone Points, regardless of order. When a heist is converted to a quest journal, the 4 points sit on four milestone events (1 point each). All four heists can be run in any order at any point after **Faction Outposts**. Track cumulative points against the Milestone Points progression table (L5 at 13, L6 at 18, L7 at 23). The pace is order-agnostic: the *first* completed heist reaches level 5, the *second* reaches level 6, and level 7 requires *all four*, because three heists leave the total one point short. One of the four heists is always Kolat Towers, so level 7 is unreachable without it.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:330: This quest's transition is open. The three remaining lair heists (**Cassalanter Villa**, **Sea Maidens Faire**, **Kolat Towers**) are available in any order, and the Stone's sharpened impressions plus faction debrief intelligence provides organic guidance toward each. The Founders' Day deadline creates pressure without prescribing order.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:342: - **Cassalanter Villa** — Stone 1-Eye impressions (Sea Ward villa, hidden temple) point toward **Cassalanter Villa**. Cassalanter faction state reflects what the party's Cassalanter relationship has been.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:344: - **Kolat Towers** — Samara's testimony (X7), Nihiloor's Manshoon intelligence (X23), and a third clue seeded in **Cassalanter Villa** form the Three Clue Rule paths to **Kolat Towers**.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:410: The Manshoon intelligence in X23 is a designed cross-quest clue. Three independent clue paths lead to **Kolat Towers**: Samara's testimony in X7 (a tower in the North Ward), Nihiloor's intelligence files in X23 (partial Kolat Towers layout and lieutenant roster), and a third path seeded in **Cassalanter Villa**. The Three Clue Rule is satisfied across quests, not within a single location. This is the correct application of the rule in a campaign where quests run in variable order.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:1: # Cassalanter Villa
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:9: Three years before the Trollskull fireball, Victoro and Ammalia Cassalanter signed a contract with Asmodeus. The terms were specific: Osvaldo, their eldest, already nine, the age at which the contract claims each child — taken immediately, transformed into a chain devil, bound. The twins, Terenzio and Elzerina — souls forfeit on their ninth birthday, one tenday after Founders' Day. The buy-out clause: 999,999 gold pieces and the sacrifice of ninety-nine souls, delivered before the twins' birthday. The Cassalanters had traded their children for time they could not afford and power they could not refuse.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:11: The desperation began with Dagult Neverember. His embezzlement from the city treasury gutted the Cassalanter banking operations — loans underwritten against civic funds that no longer existed. Victoro's investments collapsed in sequence. The family's reputation held because reputation lags reality, but the ledgers told a different story. Ammalia found the path through infernal research: a patron who could restore their fortune and guarantee their children's futures. The contract arrived through a pit fiend intermediary. They signed it together, in the temple beneath their own villa, on a night when Osvaldo was sleeping two floors above.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:13: Osvaldo's transformation was immediate. The boy screamed for three hours before the change completed. Victoro sealed the attic with abjuration wards, soundproofed the floor, and has not entered the room since. Ammalia reads to her son weekly through the warded door — children's stories he loved before the contract, spoken to a chain devil who weeps black ichor and claws at the walls. Two imps in spider form watch the corridor. The household staff knows the attic is forbidden. Only Willifort Crowelle — the doppelganger butler who serves Asmodeus directly — knows what is behind the door.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:15: Eye #2 came to the Cassalanters through infernal divination. A commune ritual performed in the temple — Asmodeus answering through a pit fiend proxy — identified the Eye's resting place: the crypt of Lady Alethea Brandath, Renaer Neverember's mother, interred in the Brandath family mausoleum beneath the City of the Dead. Victoro dispatched Seffia Naelryke's team to extract it. The Eye now rests behind the base of the Asmodeus statue in A7, the ceremonial hall of the temple beneath the villa — warded by infernal magic, guarded by the cult's bearded devils, secured in the one place the Cassalanters control absolutely.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:17: The villa above is Waterdeep's most admired household. Ammalia's charity in the Field Ward feeds hundreds. Victoro's banking operations underwrite half the guilds in the city. The children attend social functions, well-dressed and well-mannered, accompanied by their caretaker Laiba Rosse. The butterfly garden hosts dueling nobles and garden parties. The ballroom floor is a Siamorphe mosaic, and the Cassalanters maintain an ancestral devotion to the god of nobility — publicly. Beneath that floor, down through the wine cellar and a secret door marked with a sun symbol, a spiral stair descends thirty feet to a temple where midnight-every-tenday gatherings see crimson-robed cultists in golden masks kneeling before a thirty-foot statue of Asmodeus carved from what was once the image of Siamorphe herself.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:19: Founders' Day falls on the first of Flamerule. Ammalia has prepared ten vials of midnight tears poison, stored in her study beside the grimoire that contains the commune ritual. The plan: invite one hundred of Waterdeep's poorest to a charitable feast on the villa's lawn pavilions. The food is genuine. The poison is administered through the wine — ninety-nine cups, one untouched, satisfying the contract's "one shy of a hundred" requirement. The gold from the Vault of Dragons — all five hundred thousand pieces — provides the financial component. Together, gold and souls buy the twins' freedom before their birthday.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:21: The household holds six souls who know what lies beneath. Willifort Crowelle, doppelganger, serves Asmodeus with genuine devotion — the family is secondary. Tissina Khyret, Ammalia's attendant, is a cult fanatic who joined out of loyalty to Ammalia personally — she does not know the contract's full terms. Laiba "Nana" Rosse, the twins' caretaker, is a cult fanatic who joined the cult to remain close to the children — she would betray the Cassalanters to save Terenzio and Elzerina. Jandar Chergoba, head chef, is a cult fanatic bound by debt — Victoro holds a financial obligation over him that converted into cult service. Seffia Naelryke commands the external operations team and knows the cult's military posture. Arn Xalrondar, her lieutenant, handles field security for the outposts and has never set foot in the temple.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:27: **Purpose.** Translate **Faction Outposts** outpost intelligence and prior Cassalanter interactions into operational knowledge of the villa. Each faction contact provides villa-specific intelligence calibrated to what the party discovered in earlier quests. Unlike **Xanathar's Lair**'s dungeon-focused briefing, this briefing is social and political — the target looks normal from the outside.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:35: *Mirt (Harpers — renown 1+).* Mirt has attended Cassalanter social events for years. He knows the villa's public layout from personal observation — the entrance hall, the ballroom, the garden, the dining rooms. He has always suspected hidden areas: "No noble family needs that much cellar space. I've been in that wine cellar — it's too small for a house that size. The stonework on the west wall doesn't match." If the Asmodean Shrine outpost was hit in **Faction Outposts**, Mirt can confirm the cult connection and warn that Cassalanter security will have tightened. He lacks hard evidence sufficient for the Masked Lords — he needs proof from inside the villa. He says this directly: "I can suspect all I like. The Lords need something I can put on a table." At renown 10+, Mirt arranges a Harper operative — use the spy stat block — to attend the charity ball as a genuine social guest, providing a real-time outside observer with a paper bird: one communication per hour to the party inside the villa.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:37: *Jalester Silvermane (Lords' Alliance — renown 1+).* The City Watch cannot touch the Cassalanters without evidence — they are too well-connected. Jalester provides the guest list for upcoming Cassalanter social events, including the Waukeentide charity ball: names, affiliations, and which guests have standing invitations. If the party has evidence from **Faction Outposts**, Jalester can arrange Watch patrols in the Sea Ward to be "assigned elsewhere" during a specific window. He frames this as plausible deniability: "I can give you a gap. What you do with it is between you and the city." At renown 10+, Jalester pulls Watch files on the villa's recent contractor logs and delivery records — a week of staff patterns that refines the guild blueprint entry method and confirms which service entrances each contractor firm uses.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:41: *Savra Belabranta (Order of the Gauntlet — renown 1+).* The Gauntlet has suspected Cassalanter piety for months. Savra reports a secondhand account from a servant who left Cassalanter employ: screaming from above the top floor, muffled but persistent, always at night. The servant assumed rats. Savra is not convinced. If the party has evidence of Asmodean worship from **Faction Outposts**, Savra frames the temple as a holy obligation — the Order exists to destroy fiendish influence. The Order's chaplain gives the party a Siamorphe holy symbol at the end of Order of the Gauntlet Mission 5, and Order of the Gauntlet r10 is the fallback. Savra explains its significance: "Siamorphe was the Cassalanters' ancestral patron. If anything of the old faith survives in that house, this will find it." The holy symbol is the key to pacifying Caladorn's ghost in A4. At renown 10+, Savra personally attends the charity ball — Gauntlet formal dress over armor — and can suppress suspicion about one incident during the Social Event adversary roster: guests expect a knight of the Order to respond to disturbances, and her presence buys the party one improvised intervention without immediate social consequence.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:47: *Jarlaxle (Bregan D'aerthe — renown 1+).* Available only if a PC is a BD member. Jarlaxle has been watching the Cassalanters through an embedded source he does not name (Vessa, his doppelganger in the Cassalanter social circle), and he knows what the family has done. He keeps it from Bregan D'aerthe members unless they work it out themselves. He considers them "the most dangerous faction in the Grand Game because they have something none of the others have: a deadline." He will not explain what the deadline is for. He can add, if pressed, that the Cassalanters need the vault gold urgently — not from ambition, but from a fixed date on a calendar. If the party has already found evidence of Cassalanter operations through **Faction Outposts**, Jarlaxle confirms what they have rather than leading with it: "Then you've seen the edges of it. The center is underneath the villa." At renown 3+ and only with that prior evidence in hand, he provides his operative's observations on the villa's schedule and staff patterns — and adds, obliquely: "They intend to solve a problem with other people's lives. One hundred invitations. Ninety-nine cups of wine. A calculation dressed as charity." He will not explain the calculation. He does not mention "Vessa."
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:59: *Identifying the Score.* Eye #2 is in the villa's hidden temple. The Stone's impression — if 1+ Eyes are restored — sharpens to "a noble villa in the Sea Ward, hidden chambers beneath painted floors, the Cassalanter house mark on a sigil carved in stone." **Faction Outposts** documents converge on the same target: Seffia's letter from the Converted Windmill confirming Eye #2 was found "in the crypt of Lady Brandath," the Windmill's Brandath Crypt map, the Asmodean Shrine's Nine Hells mural depicting a noble family kneeling before Asmodeus. Three independent lines of evidence point at Cassalanter Villa.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:63: **Entry 1 — The Social Invitation.** If the party accepted Ammalia's dinner invitation in **Fireball!** Scene 5 and maintained a cooperative relationship, they have ongoing social access. The Cassalanters invite them to Waukeentide events as guests. Advantage: legitimate presence in the villa's public areas, ability to observe servant patterns and architecture during visits, social trust from the hosts. Disadvantage: the Cassalanters are watching. Willifort uses detect thoughts on every visitor. The party's cover depends on controlling their surface thoughts — a constraint that intensifies with every visit.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:81: **Founders' Day (Flamerule 1).** Not a heist window. This is the deadline. The poisoning feast occupies the villa's lawn pavilions. One hundred guests — the city's poorest, invited through Ammalia's Field Ward charity. Jandar prepares the food outside. Ninety-nine cups of wine contain midnight tears. The feast begins at noon. Midnight tears takes effect 24 hours after ingestion — the guests die the following day, scattered across the city, with no visible connection to the Cassalanters. If the party has not acted by this date, the sacrifice occurs.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:83: *Willifort's Surveillance.* The villa's defining social challenge. Willifort Crowelle is a doppelganger who has served the Cassalanter household for eight years. He greets every visitor at C1, offers drinks, positions himself outside doors, and uses detect thoughts continuously on anyone inside the villa. He reads surface thoughts automatically; deeper probing requires his action and a DC 15 Wisdom saving throw from the target.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:85: The party must address Willifort or accept that their surface thoughts are being monitored. Options: (a) consciously control surface thoughts — each party member declares what they are thinking about when Willifort is nearby; a DC 13 Charisma (Deception) check maintains the facade per interaction; (b) identify Willifort as a threat and neutralize him — risky, because his absence triggers an immediate household response; Ammalia checks on him within 15 minutes; (c) feed him false information deliberately — a DC 16 Charisma (Deception) check plants a specific misleading thought; Willifort reports what he reads without questioning it.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:89: *The Imp Network.* Six imps in raven form roost in the butterfly garden (C25). They observe all external approaches, track movement around the villa's perimeter, and follow visitors who linger near restricted areas. Detect magic reveals their fiendish nature (DC 12 Wisdom (Perception) to notice the eyes tracking with unnatural precision). The imps report to Ammalia through a one-way telepathic bond — she receives impressions, not words. Killing an imp alerts Ammalia immediately (the bond severs with a psychic snap). The party must account for aerial surveillance when planning any external approach or garden-adjacent movement.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:91: *Questioning Staff.* The Cassalanter household employs sixteen named staff. Ten know nothing about the temple. Six are cult members with varying degrees of commitment.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:96: - **Tissina Khyret** (cult fanatic, Ammalia's attendant) — devoted to Ammalia personally. Joined because Ammalia asked her to. Does not fully understand the contract's terms — she knows about the temple, attends gatherings, believes the worship is a family spiritual practice. If she learns the children's souls are forfeit, she breaks. DC 14 Charisma (Persuasion) with evidence of the contract turns her.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:98: - **Jandar Chergoba** (cult fanatic, head chef) — bound by debt. Victoro holds a 3,000 gp obligation over him from a failed restaurant venture. The debt converted into cult service three years ago. Jandar attends gatherings, prepares ritual meals, and commands the kitchen's ten animated knives (flying sword statistics). He hates what he does. DC 12 Charisma (Persuasion) with an offer of protection — or DC 10 if the party can resolve the debt — turns him. He provides: kitchen schedules, which deliveries access the cellar, and the location of the wine cellar's false wall.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:99: - **Seffia Naelryke** (cult operative, external operations) — commands the Cassalanter response team. Present in the villa only during cult gatherings or when summoned. True believer in Asmodean hierarchy. Will not turn.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:105: - **Pela** (laundress, halfling female, 50s) — works in the service wing. Has found crimson-stained fabric in the household laundry — she assumed it was wine. Knows that Ammalia's study (C22) is always locked and that the key never leaves Ammalia's person. If told the truth: terrified, will not actively help but will not impede the party.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:106: - **Marchello** (gardener, human male, 30s) — maintains the butterfly garden. Knows the ravens (imps) are unnaturally attentive — "They watch you. Regular birds don't watch you." Has not connected this to anything supernatural. Tends the garden at dawn and dusk. If told the truth: angry, specifically at Ammalia ("She told me those were rescue birds from the docks").
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:109: - **Lissette** (upstairs maid, human female, 30s) — cleans the family's private rooms on the second and third floors. Knows that Ammalia cries after visiting the top floor — she has heard her through the door. Has never been to the attic. Knows that C6 is sealed by more than a lock — she has seen the helmed horror through a crack when Victoro left the door ajar. If told the truth: conflicted loyalty to the children; provides the second-floor key rotation.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:135: **Caladorn Cassalanter's Ghost (A4/A4a).** In the family crypt beneath the temple, the ghost of Victoro's father — a former Masked Lord, a hero of the North, and a devoted servant of Siamorphe — is bound with three specters (former Cassalanter ancestors). Caladorn's ghost attacks any creature that bears the taint of fiendish worship — the Cassalanters cannot reach him. They reshaped his family crypt's goddess-statue into Asmodeus using stone shape. His ghost has been raging in silence for three years, unable to leave A4a, unable to stop what his family has done to his legacy.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:137: If a character presents a Siamorphe holy symbol (from the Order's chaplain at the end of Order of the Gauntlet Mission 5, or from Savra at Order of the Gauntlet r10), the specters in A4 stand down. Caladorn's ghost manifests in A4a — an armored figure with the Cassalanter features, silver-haired, carrying the family sword. He is lucid — steady voice, measured words, until he mentions his family. Then the voice cracks.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:147: **Path 1 — Help the Cassalanters Find the Gold (Cooperative).** The buy-out clause requires 999,999 gold pieces and ninety-nine souls. The Cassalanters have approximately 500,000 gold from their own fortune. They need the Vault of Dragons' 500,000 GP to complete the financial component. If the party discovers the full truth — the contract, the children, the deadline — and approaches the Cassalanters with this knowledge, the Cassalanters are open to cooperation. They are desperate parents, not ideological devil-worshippers. Victoro drops the social mask. Ammalia weeps. They explain the terms and insist the sacrifice is the only way — but a DC 12 Wisdom (Insight) check reveals they would accept any alternative that saves their children. The party can propose: help get the Vault gold in exchange for the Eye and a cessation of cult activities. The Cassalanters agree without hesitation. Cost: the entire Vault goes to Asmodeus. The party receives no gold from Neverember's hoard. And the ninety-nine — the Cassalanters will still attempt the feast unless the party explicitly addresses this. The sacrifice clause is separate from the gold clause.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:149: **Path 2 — Destroy the Infernal Contract (Mace of Disruption).** The Cassalanters want this — they have searched for years and found nothing. The answer is in their own temple: the contract is bound to the Asmodeus statue in A7. Caladorn Cassalanter's ghost in A4a has the weapon that can do it — the mace of disruption. Striking the statue with the mace cracks it, shatters the sacrificial fire bowl (2d10 psychic damage to witnessing cultists), severs the contract, and releases Eye #2 from behind the statue's base. The children's souls are freed without the gold payment or the ninety-nine sacrifices. Osvaldo's chain devil form dissolves — he dies, but his soul is freed.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:162: - **Ammalia's grimoire in C22** — contains the commune ritual, the contract's terms, and Ammalia's marginal notes ("There must be another way. There must be.").
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:163: - **Victoro's journal in C4** — entries spanning three years: "Neverember's dragons are an answer to our prayers." "The Cassalanter mausoleum. Inform Y.G." (Yalah Gralhund). "Golorr is an aboleth. Now it all makes sense."
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:168: Victoro and Ammalia do not fight. They break. Ammalia weeps. Victoro drops the social mask entirely — the polished banker vanishes and a frightened father remains. They explain the full terms: 999,999 GP and 99 souls. They insist the sacrifice is the only way. If the party asks "What if we destroy the contract?" — the Cassalanters are stunned. They have searched for years and found nothing. If the party has already discovered the mace from Caladorn's ghost, the Cassalanters are overwhelmed — the solution was beneath their own villa, in a crypt they desecrated, guarded by a ghost who will not speak to them. If the party has not yet found the mace, the Cassalanters beg them to search the temple: "There must be something down there we missed. We can never speak to Caladorn's spirit — he attacks us on sight." If the party asks about the ninety-nine, the Cassalanters go quiet. Ammalia looks away. Victoro says: "We have tried everything else."

campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:191: **C1 — Entrance Hall.** Two-story foyer with marble floors and a grand staircase. Portraits of Cassalanter ancestors line the walls — Caladorn is prominently placed, silver-haired and armored. Willifort greets every visitor here, offering to take coats and provide refreshments. During social events: a harpsichord plays in the corner, chatting guests fill the space, and Willifort circulates with a tray. The party's social exposure stage begins here — Willifort's detect thoughts is active from the moment they enter.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:195: **C4 — Reading Room.** Ground floor, east wing. A reading room with leather chairs and a black dragon hunting trophy mounted on the wall. Victoro's private journal is on a side table — he reads it here and sometimes forgets to take it with him. The journal contains three entries relevant to the investigation: "Neverember's dragons are an answer to our prayers" (confirming the gold is the buy-out). "The Cassalanter mausoleum. Inform Y.G." (Yalah Gralhund — cross-references the **Gralhund Villa** connection). "Golorr is an aboleth. Now it all makes sense" (confirming Victoro understands the Stone's nature).
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:197: **C6 — Victoro's Office.** Second floor, locked (DC 17 Dexterity (Thieves' Tools) or Victoro's key). A helmed horror stands in the corner — motionless until a creature other than Victoro or Ammalia touches the desk, at which point it attacks without warning. The desk contains: thirteen loan contracts (Cassalanter banking leverage over Waterdeep's guilds), a hidden compartment (DC 15 Intelligence (Investigation)) holding a gold Asmodeus holy symbol worth 250 GP, a report from Seffia Naelryke on outpost operations (cross-reference to **Faction Outposts** Converted Windmill), and the Cassalanter Report on the Grand Game. The Report on the Grand Game is the Three Clue Rule path to **Kolat Towers** — it references Manshoon's operations, identifies Kolat Towers by name and location, describes Zhentarim splinter movements, and notes that "the wizard who calls himself Manshoon guards his fortress with a force field that admits only those he has keyed."
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:203: **C22 — Ammalia's Study.** Third floor, locked (DC 18 Dexterity (Thieves' Tools) or Ammalia's key). A red leather grimoire sits on a lectern — the commune ritual to contact Asmodeus (who responds through a pit fiend proxy), the contract's terms transcribed in Ammalia's handwriting, and marginal notes in increasing desperation. The desk holds Ammalia's spellbook and ten vials of midnight tears poison. A trapdoor in the floor opens to C23 (a closet leading to C24, the attic). The Cassalanter Report on the Grand Game is also here per the Alexandrian overlay — a second placement of the **Kolat Towers** clue document, ensuring that parties who reach C22 but not C6 still find the path to Kolat Towers.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:229: **A4 / A4a — Family Crypt / Caladorn's Crypt.** Twelve sarcophagi in a vaulted chamber. Three specters rise from the floor when a living creature enters — they attack unless shown a Siamorphe holy symbol, at which point they retreat into their sarcophagi and become inert. Beyond A4, a smaller chamber (A4a) holds Caladorn Cassalanter's sarcophagus and his ghost.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:233: **A7 — Ceremonial Hall.** The temple's heart. A thirty-foot statue of Asmodeus carved from what was once a Siamorphe figure — the proportions are wrong in ways that suggest alteration rather than original design. A sacrificial fire bowl sits at the statue's base (contact: 4d10 fire damage). During cult gatherings: thirty cultists in crimson robes and golden masks, Victoro leading the rite from the dais, Ammalia pouring wine, six invisible imps perched on the statue's shoulders.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:243: Victoro (9th-level spellcaster, see the **Bestiary** (not yet drafted)) occupies C4 or C6 during daylight hours; during cult gatherings, he leads the rite in A7. His combat behavior: spiritual weapon and hold person to control and divide, shield of faith on Ammalia if she is present, banishment on Ammalia if reduced to 30 HP or fewer (sending her to the Ethereal Plane for safety). He does not fight to kill initially — he uses magic to subdue.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:245: Ammalia (9th-level spellcaster, see the **Bestiary** (not yet drafted)) occupies C22 or the second-floor family rooms during daylight hours; during gatherings, she is in A7. Her combat behavior: charm person (5th-level slot, up to 5 targets) to defuse confrontation, dominate person on the most dangerous combatant, counterspell defensively. She arrives to aid Victoro within 2 rounds of hearing combat. She does not fight to kill unless the children are directly threatened.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:259: 40–60 civilian guests fill C1, C5 lower, C9, C13, C15, and C25. Ten servant commoners circulate through service areas. Guards at C5 reinforced to 4. Willifort circulates with a tray. Ammalia moves between C13 (ballroom), C15 (banquet hall), and C25 (garden) on a 20-minute rotation. Victoro holds position in C9 (dining room) or C4 (reading room), entertaining guests. "Vessa" the BD operative is in C15. Esvele Rosznar stages in C2. Jandar is in C10 with full kitchen staff. The helmed horror in C6 is active regardless of event status. Temple layer is sealed during social events — the C28 secret door is closed, and no cult activity occurs.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:273: **Stage 3 — Suspected.** Ammalia engages the party in pointed conversation — charm person (5th-level slot, up to 5 targets, DC 15 Wisdom saving throw). She frames it as hospitality: "You seem so tense. Let me get you something to drink. Tell me what's really brought you here tonight." Guards take positions at all stairwells and the cellar door. Private areas are locked and watched. Jandar is positioned at the kitchen door. The party can still recover — but only through a dramatic pivot: revealing what they know about the children forces a confrontation (see the Cooperative Discovery Path in Scene 3), or a DC 18 Charisma (Deception) Group Check convinces the Cassalanters that the suspicious behavior was a misunderstanding.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:277: **Stage 4 — Exposed.** The party's true intentions are known. Guards mobilize. Willifort drops the tiefling disguise. Ammalia attempts dominate person (DC 15 Wisdom saving throw) on the party member she considers most dangerous. The C28/C29 passage is sealed — Victoro uses arcane lock. The party must fight, flee, or make a dramatic pivot: revealing knowledge of the children's souls and the contract terms forces the confrontation scene from the Cooperative Discovery Path. At Stage 4, this pivot is the party's last chance at a non-combat resolution. The Cassalanters will listen — even now — if the party can prove they know the truth and offer a way out.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:285: **Purpose.** What goes wrong, what goes sideways, and how the party gets out. The Esvele Rosznar parallel heist fires here. The Cassalanter confrontation plays out if it has not already occurred in Scene 4.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:303: Both are spellcasters. Mutually devoted — each arrives to aid the other within 2 rounds of hearing combat. They do not fight to kill initially: Ammalia opens with charm person and dominate person to divide and subdue; Victoro uses hold person and spiritual weapon to control the field. If Victoro is reduced to 30 HP or fewer, he casts banishment on Ammalia (sending her to the Ethereal Plane for 1 minute of safety). Both negotiate if reduced — they do not fear arrest (they have wealth, connections, and legal resources). They fear the Founders' Day clock. They plead with the party to let them finish saving the children.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:305: The confrontation should feel tragic. These are desperate parents who chose a monstrous path. Ammalia reading to her chain-devil son. Victoro unable to visit the attic. Elzerina stepping between Terenzio and anything that frightens him. These details humanize without excusing.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:321: **Purpose.** Resolve the heist. Insert Eye #2. Trigger the Stone of Golorr's knowledge expansion. Set the Cassalanter faction state. Deliver the all-seven-faction debriefs, branched by outcome. Update the Founders' Day clock.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:343: **Cassalanters — set the "Cassalanter active?" flag.** Five states:
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:351: *Exposed.* Evidence delivered to the Masked Lords. Cassalanters arrested or fled. Cult disbanded. Children's souls forfeit on their birthday — Asmodeus claims them. They become lemures. Victoro and Ammalia vow to destroy those who thwarted them (from prison or exile). Banking operations disrupted, affecting guild debts across the city.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:370: - *Savra (Order of the Gauntlet).* Exultant. Caladorn Cassalanter was a hero of the North — his ghost's holy weapon destroyed an infernal altar. The Gauntlet's Siamorphe symbol was instrumental. If the Cassalanters survive and are redeemed: "They were damned, and you undamned them. That is the Order's highest calling." She wants to know if the family will publicly repent. If they do, the Gauntlet may become an unexpected Cassalanter ally — watching over the family's redemption.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:395: *Founders' Day Clock.* This quest consumes 1–2 tendays total: several days of intelligence gathering and casing (Scenes 1–3), 1–3 days for the operation and escape (Scenes 4–5), and 2–4 days of recovery and debrief (Scene 6). Update the campaign calendar. The GM communicates the advancing deadline through environmental detail: Founders' Day preparations visible across the city, Cassalanter-funded pavilions being erected in the Field of Triumph, festival decorations going up in every ward, and — if the party has Cassalanter contacts — increasingly strained behavior from Ammalia.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:397: *Transition.* The party holds the Stone of Golorr and 1–2 Eyes. The children's fate is resolved or ticking. The remaining lair heists — whichever of **Xanathar's Lair**, **Sea Maidens Faire**, and **Kolat Towers** have not been completed — are available in any order. The Cassalanter Report on the Grand Game (C6/C22) points toward **Kolat Towers**. Esvele's Viper Den (if discovered) points toward the Brandath Crypts (**Vault of Dragons**). The Stone's impressions guide the party toward remaining targets.

campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:411: **Xanathar's Lair**, **Cassalanter Villa**, **Sea Maidens Faire**, and **Kolat Towers** each award the same 4 Milestone Points, regardless of order. When a heist is converted to a quest journal, the 4 points sit on four milestone events (1 point each). All four heists can be run in any order at any point after **Faction Outposts**. Track cumulative points against the Milestone Points progression table (L5 at 13, L6 at 18, L7 at 23). The pace is order-agnostic: the *first* completed heist reaches level 5, the *second* reaches level 6, and level 7 requires *all four*, because three heists leave the total one point short. One of the four heists is always Kolat Towers, so level 7 is unreachable without it.

campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:443: # Design Notes: Cassalanter Villa
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:453: The system uses staged escalation rather than binary pass/fail because a single failed Deception check should not end the heist. A party that fails one check at Stage 1 gets noticed — Willifort pays more attention, guards shift. A party that fails again gets suspected — Ammalia intervenes with charm magic, doors lock. A party that fails a third time is exposed — the social layer collapses and combat begins. But even at Stage 4, the party has one final out: revealing what they know about the children forces the confrontation scene from the Cooperative Discovery Path. This is deliberate. The social exposure system is designed to create compounding tension, not a cliff edge. Each stage gives the party a chance to recover, adapt, or pivot. The party that panics and fights at Stage 2 had other options. The party that reaches Stage 4 and plays the children card has earned a dramatic moment.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:459: This quest's four resolution paths exist because the Cassalanter story is a trolley problem with no clean solution. The "right" legal action — expose the cult — kills the children. Helping the Cassalanters save the children requires either paying Asmodeus or ignoring ninety-nine murdered innocents. Destroying the contract frees the children but invites infernal retribution and eliminates the family's patron. Every path has a cost. No path is clean.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:467: The Alexandrian places Eye #2 in Victoro's desk (C6). Moving it to the temple's ceremonial hall (A7) is a deliberate structural decision. If the Eye were in C6, the party could complete the heist entirely within the villa's social layer — pick the lock, defeat the helmed horror, take the Eye, leave. The temple would be optional. The temple is not optional. It is where the truth lives — the statue, the sacrificial bowl, the crypt of the family that was. The two-layer heist structure forces the party through both phases: social infiltration to reach the cellar, then dungeon descent to reach the Eye. This is the correct structure for a quest whose dramatic question is about what lies beneath the surface.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:485: The confrontation is written to feel tragic rather than triumphant because the Cassalanters are not cackling villains. They are parents who chose a monstrous path to save their children. The details that humanize them — Ammalia reading to a chain devil, Victoro unable to visit the attic, Elzerina stepping between Terenzio and anything that frightens him — exist to make the judgment hard.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:499: The Cassalanter Report on the Grand Game is placed in both C6 (Victoro's desk) and C22 (Ammalia's study). This dual placement ensures that a party reaching either room — not necessarily both — discovers the path to Kolat Towers. The report references Manshoon by operational name, identifies the Towers' location, describes Zhentarim splinter activity, and notes the force-field entry restriction. It is the third of three independent paths to **Kolat Towers**, alongside Samara's testimony from **Xanathar's Lair** X7 and Nihiloor's intelligence files from **Xanathar's Lair** X23.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:501: The Three Clue Rule is now satisfied across quests. A party that skipped **Xanathar's Lair** entirely can still reach **Kolat Towers** through this quest. A party that completed **Xanathar's Lair** but missed both the Samara and Nihiloor paths can reach **Kolat Towers** through the Cassalanter report. No single failed check, missed room, or skipped quest blocks all three paths. This is the correct application of the Three Clue Rule in a campaign where lair heists run in variable order.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:507: This is more severe than **Xanathar's Lair**'s scaling (where higher tiers added guards and traps but did not change the fundamental approach) because social infiltration depends on the target being socially open. A villa that is closed to visitors cannot be socially infiltrated. The party that hit both Cassalanter outposts in **Faction Outposts** traded social access for intelligence — a real cost that the difficulty table makes legible.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:511: Founders' Day matters more in this quest than in any other because it is not just a calendar marker — it is the sacrifice deadline. If the party pursues this quest late in the campaign, Founders' Day may have already passed, and the sacrifice with it. The aftermath handles both the "prevented" and "it already happened" states, but the GM should make the approaching deadline felt. Festival preparations in the streets, Cassalanter pavilions going up, staff discussing catering — these are environmental clock signals that the party can read.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:527: The conditional handling (this is Eye #1 vs. Eye #2) ensures the scene works regardless of quest order. A party that runs **Cassalanter Villa** before **Xanathar's Lair** gets the 1-Eye awakening here; a party that runs **Xanathar's Lair** first gets the 2-Eye upgrade. Both are written in full. The GM does not need to interpolate.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:533: **Lords' Alliance.** The Vhaspar Holmbridge investigation from Mission 4's Aftermath produces an early Cassalanter dossier Jalester can share in Scene 1: one complete Watch guard rotation for the villa's exterior and a partial layout of the villa grounds. This is a genuine casing advantage — one confirmed checkpoint and rough perimeter dimensions before Scene 2 begins. The dossier is conditional on Mission 4's Aftermath having played out; if Mission 4 was skipped, the Lords' Alliance has no advance intelligence on the villa. Full mission details: the Quest Hooks section of the **Lords' Alliance** Factions guide page.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:535: **Emerald Enclave.** The Cassalanter infernal ritual is disrupting a ley line running through the Sea Ward beneath Phaulkonmere. Jeryth's gardens show physical interference — herbs dying overnight, birds abandoning estate oaks — that corresponds to the ritual's operating schedule. She can detect the surge pattern; given this quest's timeline, those surges function as a natural calendar indicator for Founders' Day (Flamerule 1) without requiring the party to already know the deadline. If the party asks Melannor why the Enclave cares about the Cassalanters, this is the answer. Full mission details: the Quest Hooks section of the **Emerald Enclave** Factions guide page.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:537: **Force Grey.** Vajra holds documentation on the Cassalanter infernal contract obtained through Thayan channels she will not name. It is more detailed than the Harpers' version — provenance, specific terms — but she shares it only if the party commits to recovering the original contract from the villa rather than destroying it. "A copy tells me what was agreed. The original tells me what was actually meant." If the party has also accepted Mirt's recovery demand, they now have two principals expecting the same artifact. Full mission details: the Quest Hooks section of the **Force Grey** Factions guide page.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:25: **Purpose.** Translate **Faction Outposts** outpost intelligence and prior Jarlaxle interactions into actionable fleet knowledge. Each faction contact provides ship-specific intelligence calibrated to what the party already discovered. Same format as **Xanathar's Lair** Scene 1 and **Cassalanter Villa** Scene 1: a montage of faction consultations, each adding a piece to the picture.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:31: Unlike **Xanathar's Lair** (where the briefing confirms a known dungeon) or **Cassalanter Villa** (where the briefing describes a villa that looks normal from outside), this briefing reveals that the target is itself a deception. Every contact is pointing at the same fleet, but none of them fully understand what it is. The party should leave this scene feeling that they are casing something that does not want to be cased — a carnival that is also a military installation, operated by people who have not been found out by any of them.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:47: **Tone.** Convergent intelligence with a note of uncertainty. Unlike **Xanathar's Lair** (where the target is a known dungeon) or **Cassalanter Villa** (where the target looks normal from outside), this briefing reveals that the target is a deception — and the party must decide how deep the deception runs before they can plan against it. Every contact is pointing at the same fleet, but none of them fully understand what it is.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:87: **Tone.** Reconnaissance with a carnival veneer. The party is casing a target that looks like fun — bright colors, music, crowds, exotic spectacles. Beneath the surface, the same methodical intelligence-gathering as **Xanathar's Lair** and **Cassalanter Villa**, but dressed in greasepaint. The contrast between the carnival atmosphere and the covert military operation underneath is the scene's defining texture.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:160: **J30 (Zardoz Zord's Cabin).** Jarlaxle's private quarters aboard the Eyecatcher. 1 nimblewright valet. Contains: the Nimblewright Sales Ledger (complete list of nine buyers — cross-reference **Fireball!** Scene 4 for the partial ledger the party may already hold); the Report on the Cultists of Asmodeus (identifies the Asmodean Shrine on Aveen Street and the ancient worship site below the Sea Ward — Three Clue Rule path to **Cassalanter Villa**, if not yet completed); Jarlaxle's Report on the Grand Game (references all four factions' positions including Manshoon's Kolat Towers — Three Clue Rule path to **Kolat Towers**). The trapdoor to the Scarlet Marpenoth is beneath a rug in the aft corner — DC 16 Perception to locate, DC 18 Thieves' Tools to unlock or Jarlaxle's key.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:184: *The Three-Beat Improvisation Structure.* This quest does not use **Xanathar's Lair**'s four-stage alarm cascade or **Cassalanter Villa**'s four-stage social exposure system. Instead:
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:253: **If this is Eye #1 (neither Xanathar's Lair nor Cassalanter Villa completed).** The Stone awakens for the first time. A voice — neither masculine nor feminine, faintly resonant with something older than the gemstone itself — speaks for the first time: "Two remain." The fragmentary impressions clarify into two distinct images: a beholder's unblinking eye in deep darkness, carved stone wet from underground springs; and a Sea Ward villa with a sun symbol marking a hidden door, the Cassalanter house mark pressed into the stone above a spiral stair. The Stone does not reveal vault location, command phrase, or the nature of its own consciousness. It says "Two remain," and then it is quiet.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:255: **If this is Eye #2 (one prior lair heist completed — either Xanathar's Lair or Cassalanter Villa).** The Stone's voice deepens, and the quality of its attention changes. It is more present. It says "One remains," and then it does something it has not done before — it describes what it knows. The final Eye's location resolves fully:
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:257: - *If **Xanathar's Lair** was completed first:* "A villa in the Sea Ward. A temple beneath it, stone floor engraved with the Cassalanter house mark. The children's laughter has a hollow quality — it comes from a room that has been sealed for three years."
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:258: - *If **Cassalanter Villa** was completed first:* "A beholder's eye, unblinking in darkness. The lair beneath Skullport. A paranoid mind that trusts nothing and no one — but trusts the fishbowl."
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:262: **If this is Eye #3 (both Xanathar's Lair and Cassalanter Villa already completed).** The Stone goes still. Then it speaks with sudden, precise clarity — the fragmentary impressions are gone. The vault's location does not resolve as an address but as a riddle in the Stone's alien cadence — imagery and verse pointing toward the Brandath Crypts, requiring interpretation to reach, and the Stone gives the command phrase with it. The guardian's nature is described with the clinical precision of a being that has watched a gold dragon stand alone in the dark with five hundred thousand gold coins for years: bound by oath, loyal, patient beyond mortal reckoning, extraordinarily dangerous.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:282: **Cassalanters.** If **Cassalanter Villa** already completed, no change. If not, imp surveillance from the Asmodean Shrine may have observed harbor activity.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:302: *Transition.* The party holds the Stone with 1–3 Eyes. The remaining lair heists are available in any order. The Report on Cultists of Asmodeus (J30) points toward **Cassalanter Villa** if not yet completed. The Letter from Nar'l (U3) confirms the BD/Xanathar connection, providing context for **Xanathar's Lair**. Jarlaxle's Report on the Grand Game (J30) and the Zhentarim pass-amulets (if recovered from Scene 5) both point toward **Kolat Towers**. If this was Eye #3 — the Full Awakening — **Vault of Dragons** launches immediately.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:304: **Tone.** Decompression weighted with consequence. The caper's resolution carries a different emotional register than **Xanathar's Lair**'s revelatory awakening or **Cassalanter Villa**'s moral reckoning. This quest's aftermath is about relationships — with Jarlaxle (enemy, rival, or ally?), with Zelifarn (the crystal ball decision), with Manshoon's Zhentarim (confirmed threat, now owed payback). The party has engaged with the most charming villain in the Grand Game. What that engagement produced depends entirely on how they played it.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:316: **Xanathar's Lair**, **Cassalanter Villa**, **Sea Maidens Faire**, and **Kolat Towers** each award the same 4 Milestone Points, regardless of order. When a heist is converted to a quest journal, the 4 points sit on four milestone events (1 point each). All four heists can be run in any order at any point after **Faction Outposts**. Track cumulative points against the Milestone Points progression table (L5 at 13, L6 at 18, L7 at 23). The pace is order-agnostic: the *first* completed heist reaches level 5, the *second* reaches level 6, and level 7 requires *all four*, because three heists leave the total one point short. One of the four heists is always Kolat Towers, so level 7 is unreachable without it.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:328: This quest's transition is open. The remaining lair heists are available in any order. The Stone's sharpened impressions and faction debrief intelligence provide organic guidance toward each. The Tarsakh 20 departure deadline and the Founders' Day deadline (from **Cassalanter Villa**, if not yet completed) create dual pressure without prescribing order.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:341: - **Cassalanter Villa** — Report on Cultists of Asmodeus (J30) provides Three Clue Rule path to **Cassalanter Villa**. "Vessa" the BD operative in **Cassalanter Villa** previews Jarlaxle's surveillance operations.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:352: This quest must feel like a caper, not a dungeon infiltration (**Xanathar's Lair**) or a social infiltration into a dungeon (**Cassalanter Villa**). The distinction is mechanical, not just aesthetic.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:354: **Xanathar's Lair** rewards careful routing and surveillance suborning. **Cassalanter Villa** rewards maintaining cover and reading social cues. This quest rewards improvisation — the plan will go wrong, and the party's ability to adapt in the moment determines success. The three-beat improvisation structure (Something Goes Wrong → Escalation → Confrontation or Clean Escape) replaces **Xanathar's Lair**'s four-stage alarm cascade and **Cassalanter Villa**'s four-stage social exposure system. The caper genre does not use staged escalation because the drama comes from the moment of improvisation, not from a slowly tightening noose.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:386: Every lair heist needs a parallel heist actor: **Xanathar's Lair** has Jarlaxle's simultaneous heist, **Cassalanter Villa** has Esvele Rosznar, this quest has Manshoon's Zhentarim strike team. The parallel actor creates three-way pressure, provides a pressure-relief valve, and connects the current quest to future quests.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:390: The strike always fires regardless of which resolution path the party chose. Manshoon's intelligence is independent of the party's relationship with Jarlaxle. This is a deliberate departure from **Cassalanter Villa**'s Esvele matrix, where the parallel actor could be avoided entirely — Manshoon is more aggressive and more consequential.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:394: Every lair heist has a moral dimension: **Xanathar's Lair** has the smokepowder choice, **Cassalanter Villa** has the children's fate and the four resolution paths. This quest's moral dimension is smaller in scope but personal — the Nimblewright Crystal Ball in U4 belonged to Zelifarn's mother, killed by Jarlaxle's agents.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:416: This quest's departure deadline (Tarsakh 20, or Tarsakh 15 at Lockdown) is unique among lair heists. Neither **Xanathar's Lair** nor **Cassalanter Villa** has a hard deadline tied to the target physically leaving the campaign's geography. The departure deadline creates urgency of a different kind than **Cassalanter Villa**'s Founders' Day: not "something bad happens if you wait" but "the opportunity disappears." The fleet sails. The Eye goes with it.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:422: **To Cassalanter Villa (if not yet completed).** The Report on the Cultists of Asmodeus (J30) identifies the Asmodean Shrine on Aveen Street and references an ancient worship site below the Sea Ward, converging on Cassalanter Villa. This is one of three independent paths to **Cassalanter Villa**.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:426: **To Kolat Towers.** Two independent paths from this quest: Jarlaxle's Report on the Grand Game (J30) references Manshoon's operations and Kolat Towers; and the Zhentarim pass-amulets recovered from the Manshoon strike team (Scene 5) provide physical access to Kolat Towers. These join the existing paths from **Xanathar's Lair** (Samara's testimony from X7, Nihiloor's files from X23) and **Cassalanter Villa** (Cassalanter Report on the Grand Game from C6 or C22). The Three Clue Rule is satisfied to **Kolat Towers** through any combination of prior quests.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:25: **Purpose.** Convert prior-arc intelligence — the name "Manshoon," the Kolat Towers location, the pass-amulet system, the Splinter network — into an operational picture of a fortified position. Unlike **Xanathar's Lair** (a dungeon whose defenses the party can infiltrate around) or **Cassalanter Villa** (a villa with social cover) or **Sea Maidens Faire** (a carnival that looks like something other than a military installation), this briefing reveals a target that is overtly what it is: a wizard's fortress behind an impenetrable force field. The party cannot sneak up on Kolat Towers. They can only prepare better or worse for the assault.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:53: **Purpose.** Steps 1–3 of the heist framework adapted for the raid genre. Kolat Towers does not offer the same casing texture as **Xanathar's Lair** (a dungeon with accessible entry points to scout), **Cassalanter Villa** (a villa with a public face), or **Sea Maidens Faire** (a carnival in which the party can wander freely). The force field makes exterior casing the only option. The party cannot see inside the towers before the operation begins. What they can learn is: entry timing, approach windows, and force-field gap mechanics. The Identifying the Score step is unusual in this quest — no Eye, no Stone impression — which means the party's motivation must come from somewhere else entirely.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:62: - Cassalanter Report on the Grand Game (**Cassalanter Villa**): references Manshoon and identifies the towers' location in the Southern Ward, correcting Samara's misdirection.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:283: **Tone.** Tactical decompression. **Kolat Towers**'s aftermath has a different texture than the prior lair heists' endings: no revelatory awakening (**Xanathar's Lair**), no moral reckoning (**Cassalanter Villa**), no relationship reassessment (**Sea Maidens Faire**). This is strategic positioning — the party has done a violent thing that was necessary, has recovered intelligence that changes the **Vault of Dragons** situation, and has set a faction state flag that will determine what they face at the Vault. Professional satisfaction or grim assessment, depending on how the operation went.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:295: **Xanathar's Lair**, **Cassalanter Villa**, **Sea Maidens Faire**, and **Kolat Towers** each award the same 4 Milestone Points, regardless of order. When a heist is converted to a quest journal, the 4 points sit on four milestone events (1 point each). All four heists can be run in any order at any point after **Faction Outposts**. Track cumulative points against the Milestone Points progression table (L5 at 13, L6 at 18, L7 at 23). The pace is order-agnostic: the *first* completed heist reaches level 5, the *second* reaches level 6, and level 7 requires *all four*, because three heists leave the total one point short. One of the four heists is always Kolat Towers, so level 7 is unreachable without it.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:316: - **Cassalanter Villa** (Cassalanter Report on the Grand Game) — identifies Kolat Towers' Southern Ward location. Three Clue Rule path to **Kolat Towers**.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:335: **Kolat Towers** is a raid — the fourth and final genre in the lair heist sequence. **Xanathar's Lair** was dungeon infiltration (stealth and navigation). **Cassalanter Villa** was social infiltration (identity and exposure management). **Sea Maidens Faire** was a caper (improvisation and rolling adaptation). **Kolat Towers** is a raid: the party assaults a fortified position with overwhelming violence, and the primary strategic variable is speed. The reinforcement timer is not a complication added to the raid — it is the genre's defining mechanic. Every quest in the sequence tests different party capabilities. **Kolat Towers** tests combat effectiveness and decision-making under time pressure.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:339: Four quests, four genres, because the party has four different capability sets that a single-genre sequence cannot test. The raid placement at the end of the sequence is deliberate: by **Kolat Towers**, the party has demonstrated stealth capability (**Xanathar's Lair**), social intelligence (**Cassalanter Villa**), and improvisational flexibility (**Sea Maidens Faire**). The raid asks whether they can leverage all of those in a context where they cannot afford to use any of them thoughtfully — speed collapses the time for planning, and the reinforcement timer punishes hesitation. A party that treats the raid like a dungeon crawl (clearing every room before advancing) loses to the timer. A party that treats it like a caper (improvising room by room without a plan) arrives at Manshoon underpowered. The raid rewards parties who have internalized the prior quests' lessons and can execute without pausing to plan.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:343: **Kolat Towers** is the only lair heist without an Eye recovery. The structural risk is that the scene feels lower-stakes than **Xanathar's Lair**, **Cassalanter Villa**, or **Sea Maidens Faire** — that without the Stone's awakening, the climax lacks weight. The intelligence haul addresses this by giving each recovered item a specific, named **Vault of Dragons** consequence that the party understands before they enter the towers. The blackmail ledger is not "valuable treasure" — it is the political tool that neutralizes Manshoon's Masked Lord influence. The Report on the Grand Game is not "lore" — it contains the partial command phrase that opens the Vault. Every item in the haul has a downstream consequence that the party can predict, which makes recovering it feel as meaningful as restoring an Eye.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:345: The Manshoon Identity Reveal replaces the Stone's awakening as the quest's dramatic centerpiece. Where the Stone awakening in prior quests revealed something about the campaign's hidden architecture (Nihiloor's domination in **Xanathar's Lair**, the Cassalanters' debt in **Cassalanter Villa**, the aboleth consciousness in **Sea Maidens Faire**), the Identity Reveal reveals something about the antagonist's nature that recontextualizes every Manshoon interaction the party has had since **Faction Outposts**.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:369: Each lair heist has a moment that asks the party to weigh a consequence against a tactical advantage: smokepowder demolition in **Xanathar's Lair**, the children's sacrifice in **Cassalanter Villa**, Zelifarn's crystal ball in **Sea Maidens Faire**. **Kolat Towers**'s equivalent is the Retributive Strike Gambit. If the party is winning the fight against Manshoon, they must decide how to prevent him from breaking the staff — which means either disarming him while he's still dangerous enough to threaten the gambit, or accepting the explosion, or finding a third path (negotiation that takes him past his desperation threshold without combat reaching 30 HP). The gambit compresses **Kolat Towers**'s moral dimension into a single decision point because the raid genre does not leave space for extended moral deliberation.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:33: | Eyes recovered | **Xanathar's Lair**, **Cassalanter Villa**, **Sea Maidens Faire** | Should be 3/3 entering **Vault of Dragons**. If fewer than 3, **Vault of Dragons** cannot yet be run — see Design Notes. |
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:36: | Cassalanter active? | **Cassalanter Villa** Scene 6 | Redeemed / Cooperative (Gold Delivered) / Active-Adversarial / Exposed / Founders' Day Completed (too late) |

campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:49: *Savra Belabranta (Order of the Gauntlet — renown 1+).* If the party marked **Order Recognized** in Order of the Gauntlet Mission 6 ("The Writ and the Weight"), the Halls of Justice have formally recognized the party as Order associates. Savra states plainly what that buys them: Watch officers who see the party in conflict with a Cassalanter operative will not automatically intervene, and she will personally testify on the party's behalf if any complication from the vault operation goes legal. She adds a line that has nothing to do with paperwork: "Whatever you're doing with the vault — do it fast." The Founders' Day timeline, however it resolved, has left her with the sense that Waterdeep does not have unlimited patience left for the Grand Game's loose ends. If **Order Recognized** is not marked, Savra gives the party a plain Halls of Justice letter and no testimony. A party that ran three heists plays Mission 6 only after the Vault, so this is the usual case for them. At the Final Approach she also reads each member's tithe from Order of the Gauntlet s01. **Tithe Paid** earns a greeting by name, **Tithe Paid After Asking** a nod, and **Quietly Reassigned** a note from Brother Hallin Ostrel with the gate instructions. At renown 10+, Savra positions herself at the City of the Dead's main gate during the operation, prepared to intervene if Watch response arrives before the party is ready for it.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:57: *The Stone's Role Going Forward.* The Full Awakening already happened — see below. What the Stone does for the remainder of **Vault of Dragons** is different in character from what it did in **Xanathar's Lair**, **Cassalanter Villa**, and **Sea Maidens Faire**. It is no longer withholding information; it has nothing left to reveal about the vault itself. What remains is presence. Illuun's consciousness, fully surfaced since the third Eye was seated, is close enough now that the attuned holder occasionally catches something that is not quite communication — an impression, a mood, a flicker of attention that does not belong to the Stone's usual cadence. This should be used sparingly across Scenes 2 through 4: a handful of brief, unsettling asides rather than a running commentary, timed to moments of genuine significance (the vault doors opening, the first sight of the gold) rather than every scene transition. The Stone is not the protagonist of this quest's climax. It is a passenger who has started, very quietly, to make its presence known.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:59: *The Full Awakening (Canonical Scene).* This is the scene that **Xanathar's Lair** and **Cassalanter Villa** defer to explicitly — both read, verbatim, "Run the 3-Eye disclosure scene from **Vault of Dragons** at this point." It already happened, off-page relative to **Vault of Dragons**'s own opening, in whichever quest delivered the third Eye. Use the following text at that point in whichever quest actually runs it; it should not be restaged here.

campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:212: If more than one hostile faction is present simultaneously, they do not coordinate with each other — Manshoon, Xanathar's remnants, and an Active-Adversarial Cassalanter team are all here for the same gold and are as much a threat to one another as to the party. A party that recognizes this can let its enemies do some of the fighting for it.

campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:246: *Cassalanter Resolution.* If the infernal contract was not destroyed in **Cassalanter Villa** (any state other than Redeemed), the soul-pact thread remains open here. In the Cooperative (Gold Delivered) state, the gold half of the buy-out is now complete — but the contract also requires the ninety-nine souls, and Asmodeus accepts no partial payment. If Founders' Day's sacrifice was stopped, the ritual remains incomplete regardless of the gold, and the children's souls stay forfeit until the contract is destroyed by other means. If the sacrifice happened, the children are saved and the family is a set of powerful allies stained by mass murder — a state the party helped create. An Active-Adversarial resolution that ended in the Cassalanters' defeat at the vault leaves the contract unresolved and the family exposed, cornered, and dangerous in whatever way remains available to them.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:248: If the Cassalanters were Exposed in **Cassalanter Villa** — arrested or fled before **Vault of Dragons** — the children's souls were forfeited to Asmodeus on their birthday without the infernal contract being destroyed. They became lemures. From prison or exile, Victoro and Ammalia vow to destroy those who thwarted them, but this is a long-term threat rather than a **Vault of Dragons** consequence. The party may encounter their wrath in future quests — Undermountain or beyond — but **Vault of Dragons** ends with the Cassalanters neutralized as an active faction; whatever vengeance they intend requires time, resources, and freedom they do not currently possess.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:258: **Savra Belabranta (Order of the Gauntlet).** Wants to know whether the Cassalanter matter is truly closed. If the contract still stands, she is already planning the Order's next move — this was never only about the gold for her, and she says as much.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:273: - **The Cassalanter soul-pact** (if unresolved). The infernal contract's fate continues below the city, on Undermountain's eighteenth level, at Vanrakdoom.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:292: | 3 (**Xanathar's Lair**, **Cassalanter Villa**, **Sea Maidens Faire** — Kolat Towers skipped) | 22 | 6 |
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:295: A party can only reach **Vault of Dragons** with at least three lair heists done, since all three Eyes (from **Xanathar's Lair**, **Cassalanter Villa**, **Sea Maidens Faire**) are required to open the Vault — Kolat Towers carries no Eye and is never required for entry, only for the fourth heist slot that unlocks level 7 early. This is why the quest is written for a 6th-to-7th-level party rather than a fixed 7th — Scene 5's Faction Confrontation in particular should be run with the entering level in mind (see that scene's roster-assembly table), since a level 6 party that skipped Kolat Towers also faces a full-strength, unidentified Manshoon by default (**Kolat Towers** is what typically resolves that threat).
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:323: - **Xanathar's Lair** Scene 6 / **Cassalanter Villa** Scene 6 — both defer explicitly to "the 3-Eye disclosure scene from **Vault of Dragons**." Scene 1's Full Awakening text is that scene.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:324: - **Cassalanter Villa** — Esvele's Viper Den as a backup clue path; the Cooperative (Gold Delivered) resolution's promised gold share, paid out here in Scene 5.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:343: This is the single most important practical note in the document. **Xanathar's Lair** and **Cassalanter Villa** both defer to "the 3-Eye disclosure scene from **Vault of Dragons**" without knowing which lair will actually deliver the third Eye — the party's own choices determine that. **Sea Maidens Faire**, published earlier in the drafting sequence, wrote the disclosure inline rather than deferring, on the theory that a scene this significant deserved to exist wherever it naturally lands rather than as a forward reference to a document that didn't exist yet at the time. The result is a minor structural seam: **Sea Maidens Faire**'s inline text and this document's canonical text needed to agree closely enough that a GM reading both back to back would not notice a difference. They do. If **Vault of Dragons** is being run and the third Eye came from **Xanathar's Lair** or **Cassalanter Villa**, use Scene 1's Full Awakening text as written — it is the canonical version, and it was written to match **Sea Maidens Faire**'s language precisely so that whichever quest actually delivers the disclosure, the moment reads identically. Do not restage the disclosure a second time in **Vault of Dragons**'s own Scene 1 if it already happened in Scene 6 of whichever prior quest delivered the third Eye — reference that it happened, briefly, and move on.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:365: **Xanathar's Lair** through **Kolat Towers** each calibrate their difficulty against a single lair's escalation tier — how aggressively the party moved through **Faction Outposts**'s outposts, essentially. **Vault of Dragons** has no equivalent fresh escalation clock, because it doesn't need one: the Faction-State Summary Table already carries every difficulty variable the campaign has generated across its entire length. A party that dismantled Manshoon's Splinter in **Kolat Towers**, redeemed the Cassalanters in **Cassalanter Villa**, and allied with Jarlaxle in **Sea Maidens Faire** arrives at Scene 5 facing, essentially, nobody. A party that skipped **Kolat Towers**, left the Cassalanters Active-Adversarial, and made an enemy of Bregan D'aerthe arrives facing all three at once. This is not a punishment mechanic bolted onto the finale — it is the entire campaign's accumulated weight, made visible in a single afternoon, which is exactly what a convergence quest should do.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:373: The source material gestures at a contingency for a party that reaches this point with a damaged or incomplete Stone, but does not supply a mechanical framework for it beyond acknowledging the possibility exists. This document assumes the standard path: three Eyes recovered across **Xanathar's Lair**, **Cassalanter Villa**, and **Sea Maidens Faire**, in any order. A table that has genuinely lost an Eye to destruction rather than simple non-recovery is in territory this document does not cover, and inventing an elaborate degraded-vault mechanic for a scenario the source material itself only gestures at would add more complexity than the edge case warrants. If it comes up, the simplest fix is usually the right one: let the party find another way to the same information through legwork, at a DC and a time cost the GM judges fair, rather than building a parallel ceremony for a version of the Stone that was never fully realized in the source design.
campaign/structure/ch1-beginning.md:275: **Flaws** are the character's internal arc — each one a thing the character must overcome by the end of the campaign to become who they need to be. These are genuine internal obstacles, not personality flavoring or DM tools. Each one answers: *what is this character's damage, and what does it cost them?* The campaign's events — the fireball, the Cassalanter children, the vault, the gold — are designed to press on exactly these.

campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:144: Inter-faction activity continues off-screen. If the PCs spend three sessions on Cassalanter outposts, Xanathar and Manshoon have not been idle. The DM should have a rough answer for what each faction did during that time if the PCs investigate.
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:216: | Order of the Gauntlet | Active suppression of evil; direct action over subtlety | Combat support; assault on known villain lairs; the Cassalanter sacrifice prevented |
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:264: Ammalia Cassalanter's deadline does not appear in the infernal contract — it is her own calculation. The design note explains why this matters: it is a morally significant detail. The Cassalanters are not working to a devil's timetable; they are working to a mother's calculation of how fast her children's souls are eroding. This makes the deadline simultaneously more flexible (it could be wrong) and more urgent (there is no appeal). The note also explains why Founders' Day was chosen over other dates: it is late enough in the campaign year to give PCs time, early enough that it creates genuine pressure before Act IV, and its civic significance makes the sacrifice narratively resonant.
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:284: 1. **The Founders' Day resolution:** The DM must decide before play begins which Cassalanter resolution paths they are prepared to support. Path 4 (the sacrifice happens) is a valid dramatic outcome and should not be foreclosed by soft DM reluctance — but it changes the campaign's emotional register for everything that follows. Decide in advance, not mid-session.
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:311: - Arc B: Festival calendar first becomes relevant (Twin Parades / Fleetswake, Ches 21); Founders' Day countdown implicit from Ammalia's introduction
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:314: - Arc G: Founders' Day deadline mechanics; Cassalanter resolution path selection
